- `vmec_spec()`/`airfoil_spec()` -- real adapters wrapping the two existing domain modules, with `cost_credits` computed from this project's own already-measured wall-clock numbers (airfoil low ~30ms = 1 credit, the cheapest call in the whole project; VMEC low ~5-15s = 333 credits; VMEC medium ~48s = 1600 credits; VMEC high unmeasured, `nan`, since no run has ever actually used that preset). **Verified by import and construction, not just written**: both adapters build real `DomainSpec` objects, `worker_fn` references resolve and are callable, all four task/result dataclasses construct without error.

**Triage of the 7 proposed domains against the schema** (`CANDIDATE_TRIAGE` in the same file) -- explicitly marked as confidence-rated hypotheses, not measurements, since none of the 7 were installed or run this pass (unlike TORAX, which got a real spike): Warp FEM, JAX-Fluids, and Warp Darcy are all flagged **"needs batched worker"** on the same JIT-tax hypothesis as TORAX -- medium confidence, would need the same kind of real spike to confirm. Battery/PyBaMM stands out as the closest existing conceptual fit to the ladder idea -- it already has a *named*, real fidelity hierarchy (SPM -> SPMe -> DFN -> thermal/degradation) as part of its own design, not something this project would have to invent. WISDEM and Tudat are flagged **"likely fits"** (CPU-based, not JIT-compiled) at low-medium confidence -- plausible based on how they're built, not confirmed. Photonics/Meep is flagged **unconfirmed either way** -- FDTD is a different computational shape from anything else on the list, no basis yet for a harness-fit guess.

**Open:**
- None of the 7 candidates were actually installed, run, or license-checked this pass -- the triage table is a prioritization aid for *where to spend a real spike next*, explicitly not a substitute for one (the same "confirmed by reading source, not assumed" bar TORAX was held to).
- `validity_fn` is defined in the schema but neither `vmec_spec()` nor `airfoil_spec()` actually populates it (both pass `None`) -- realizing the ok/valid split for real would mean pulling airfoil's `cd<1e-6` check out of `worker_fn` and into a standalone `validity_fn`, not done here.
- `BudgetedDiscoveryTask` has a type but no harness loop implementing it (propose -> pick fidelity -> spend -> observe -> repeat) -- the schema makes the task nameable/serializable, not runnable yet.
- Cost credits for VMEC++'s `high` fidelity preset are unmeasured (`nan`) -- that preset has never actually been called in this project.

## 28. Diffusion schedule fix, completed: full corrected numbers, and a real bootstrap-generation generalization

Closes §24[4b]/§27's open diffusion-schedule item -- retrained both `diffusion_targets_full_s0` and `airfoil_diffusion_s0` on the re-tuned linear schedule (`train_diffusion.py`'s `make_schedule`, beta_end 0.02 -> 0.12; see that file's own docstring for the full story of the cosine-schedule attempt that broke first, caught immediately, and was reverted) and reran every affected eval: the full 2-seed VMEC++ ablation grid (12 diffusion configs), the random-direction ablation (2 diffusion configs), and both airfoil diffusion configs (targeted + null). All cVAE/GAN numbers are untouched by this -- only diffusion was ever wrong.

**Corrected VMEC++ numbers, step_std=1.0, low fidelity, averaged across both seeds (all three models, same source -- `ablation_summary.json` -- for a genuinely apples-to-apples table for the first time; see the note below on why this also revises the cVAE/GAN *validity* figures quoted since §18):**

| model | validity | correct-dir (targeted) | correct-dir (null) | signal above chance | selectivity (targeted/null) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| cVAE | 62.5% | 62.2% | 46.2% | +16.0pp | 1.02 / 0.81 |
| Diffusion | 64.2% | 71.7% | 49.6% | +22.2pp | 1.22 / 0.72 |
| GAN | 73.4% | 76.3% | 49.3% | +27.0pp | 1.39 / 0.70 |

**Diffusion's aggregate signal-above-chance barely moved from the pre-fix (buggy-schedule) number** (+22.2pp now vs. ~+22.2pp pre-fix, coincidentally near-identical at the aggregate level) -- but the *individual* seed numbers underneath shifted meaningfully (e.g. seed-0 validity 58.3% vs. the old 69.2%), consistent with this project's own repeated finding (§19-20) that single-seed numbers in this eval carry roughly ±10pp of noise regardless of which schedule is underneath. The schedule bug was real and worth fixing for correctness, but it was not the thing responsible for diffusion's ranking relative to cVAE/GAN -- that ranking is unchanged: diffusion still clearly beats cVAE, GAN still beats both.

**Arbitrary-direction (random-direction) signal above chance, all three models, seed 0:**

| model | targeted mean cosine | null mean cosine | signal |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| cVAE | 0.098 | -0.026 | +0.124 |
| Diffusion | 0.128 | -0.007 | +0.135 |
| GAN | 0.207 | 0.041 | +0.166 |

Same ranking again (cVAE weakest, GAN strongest), diffusion's corrected signal (+0.135) close to but a bit below its pre-fix number (+0.208) -- within the noise band already established for this test.

**Airfoils, corrected diffusion vs. untouched cVAE/GAN:**

| model | validity | correct-dir (targeted) | correct-dir (null) | signal above chance | selectivity (targeted/null) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| cVAE | 85.8% | 68.8% | 43.8% | +25.0pp | 1.10 / 0.69 |
| Diffusion | 88.3% | 75.6% | 55.3% | +20.3pp | 2.02 / 0.87 |
| GAN | 91.7% | 86.8% | 45.0% | +41.8pp | 1.87 / 0.80 |

Diffusion's corrected airfoil validity (88.3%) is actually slightly *higher* than its pre-fix number (85.8%) -- the schedule fix didn't uniformly hurt or help, consistent with "this was a correctness fix, not a capacity change" rather than a regression.

**A real, honest side-note surfaced while pulling this table together, not swept under it**: cVAE and GAN's VMEC++ validity numbers above (62.5%, 73.4%) don't match the 55.8%/70.0% quoted since §18 for the same nominal config. Traced the cause rather than assuming either is wrong: the original 55.8%/70.0% run predates §19's `torch.manual_seed` reproducibility fix -- "the same `--seed 0`" before and after that fix are not the same draw, exactly the mechanism §19 already retracted one claim over. Both numbers are real, neither is a bug; the ablation-grid numbers in this section's table are the ones now used going forward since they're the only self-consistent, mutually-comparable source across all three models post-fix.

**Built `scripts/bootstrap_generic.py`** -- direct follow-up to a user question about generalizing dataset bootstrapping across domains. This project's three existing bootstrap tools (`bootstrap_loop.py`, `generate_and_validate.py`, `gradient_walk.py`) all predate the target-conditioned generators built from §16 onward: every one samples from `train_vae.py`'s plain *unconditional* VAE, and `bootstrap_loop.py`/`gradient_walk.py`'s "directed" search pushes toward exactly one hardcoded metric via backprop through a separate, frozen surrogate -- never the cVAE/diffusion/GAN this project has since spent §18-26 validating for actually understanding direction and not memorizing or mode-collapsing. All three also hand-roll their own copy of the subprocess-timeout harness, predating `oracle_harness.py`'s consolidation.

`bootstrap_generic.py` replaces the mechanism (not scope-expanded to replace the tools themselves yet): reuses `oracle_harness.run_batch_with_timeout` (no fourth duplicate), dispatches via `gym_schema.py`'s `vmec_spec()`/`airfoil_spec()` (the first real thing to actually consume `gym_schema.py` since §27 built it), loads any already-trained cvae/diffusion/gan checkpoint through the existing `load_generative_model` functions, and proposes candidates by pushing real anchors in **random directions in target space** (`eval_random_direction_steerability.py`'s exact direction-sampling mechanism, repurposed from evaluating steerability to driving generation) rather than one fixed metric. Two seed modes per direct user request ("start with seeds or not"): `anchor` (real row + random target-space push) and `prior` (no real anchor, target sampled directly from `N(0,1)` in z-scored target space). **Explicitly scoped out for this pass, per direct user instruction**: no retrain-the-generator-each-round loop (`bootstrap_loop.py`'s own generation structure) -- v1 reruns one fixed, already-trained checkpoint every round; retraining is a clear, deliberate follow-up, not an oversight.

Smoke-tested on both domains before calling it done (small worker counts, to avoid contending with the diffusion re-eval pipeline still running on the same 32-core machine -- the lesson from §17 about not running two 24-worker oracle batches at once, still respected): airfoil, 12/24 accepted (50.0%) in 5s; VMEC++, 7/8 accepted (87.5%) in 38s. Both ran end to end with real oracle calls, no synthetic/mocked verification.

**Open:**
- No real-scale bootstrap run yet -- only smoke-tested at n=8-24, not run to any meaningful target_count.
- `--seed-mode prior`'s aux sampling for VMEC (`nfp` uniform over `NFP_VALUES`) and airfoils (Reynolds/alpha ranges copied from `generate_airfoil_dataset.py`) are reasonable defaults, not validated against how well they actually cover useful territory.
- Retraining loop (the actual "bootstrap" in bootstrap_loop.py's sense -- warm-start retrain each generation on the growing pool) is explicitly not built, per this pass's scoping.
- `gym_schema.py`'s `validity_fn`/aux-conditioning abstraction still isn't used by this script either -- `build_round_vmec`/`build_round_airfoil` reach into `vmec_oracle`/`airfoil_oracle` and each domain's own `load_generative_model` directly, same gap §27 already flagged.

## 29. Domain-readiness audit, and the first airfoil forward scoring model

Direct user request: track every domain against a fixed 4-part bar (A: oracle, B: design->target dataset, C: forward scoring model, D: generative models) so it's clear what's actually complete versus assumed, ahead of moving generation work to a remote Slurm cluster (AICR -- connection details live in a separate session, not yet available here).

**Audited all three domains against the checkpoint inventory and scripts on disk, not from memory:**
- **VMEC++/stellarators**: A/B/C/D all complete. The most thoroughly built and audited domain by a wide margin (§1-26).
- **Airfoils/XFOIL**: A partial (one fidelity tier only, no OpenFOAM second tier), B complete, **C confirmed missing** (every `reg_mlp_*`/`reg_siren_*`/`contrastive_*` checkpoint on disk is stellarator-only -- checked directly via `ls checkpoints/`, not assumed), D complete.
- **TORAX/tokamaks**: A not built (§25's spike only), B/C/D all blocked on A. Two concrete prerequisites identified: a new Docker image (Python>=3.12/numpy>=2, incompatible with both existing images) and resolving the JIT-compile-tax/harness-fit question before committing to a harness design.

Published as a standalone artifact (status-matrix + per-domain detail + a priority order for the 7 other candidate domains from §27's triage, carried forward) -- a living reference distinct from the "Target Manifold" research-findings artifact, since this tracks infrastructure completeness, not experimental results.

**Closed the airfoil scoring-model gap**: built `scripts/train_airfoil_scoring.py` -- deliberately scoped as a first baseline (plain MLP, 137,476 params), not a port of `train.py`'s full multi-session architecture search (SIREN/attention trunks, SOAP, ensembles, cluster-split analysis) -- the same place VMEC++'s own scoring work started (§1) before that exploration happened. Same physical-sanity filter and normalization discipline as the generation scripts; random 80/10/10 split (a cluster-split generalization check, the thing that mattered enormously for VMEC++ in §6, is explicitly not done here yet -- flagged open, not assumed to transfer).

**Real result, contextualized against a naive predict-the-mean baseline** (computed directly, not assumed) rather than left as a bare RMSE number:

| target | naive (predict mean) RMSE | model RMSE | error reduction |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| cl | 0.557 | 0.072 | 87% |
| cd | 0.0166 | 0.0145 | 13% |
| cm | 0.0353 | 0.0082 | 77% |
| l_over_d | 45.28 | 9.20 | 80% |

**`cd` is the weak spot here too** -- only 13% error reduction over the naive baseline, sharply worse than the other three targets. This is the same target that showed the weakest steering signal in every generation-model result since §22 (airfoil cVAE/diffusion/GAN's correct-direction rate on `cd` was consistently the lowest or second-lowest of the four targets). Two independent directions -- forward prediction and reverse steering -- landing on the same target as the hard one is a real, converging signal that something about `cd` specifically (its own dynamic range, its log-transform, or a genuine physical property of how drag depends on this parameterization) makes it harder to model than the other three, not a coincidence of either test's own design.

**Open:**
- No cluster-split (or equivalent held-out-region) generalization check for this scoring model yet -- the random-split RMSE above should be read as an optimistic upper bound, the same caution VMEC++'s pre-§6 numbers needed.
- No architecture search done (SOAP optimizer, SIREN, ensembling) -- unknown whether any of those would move these numbers the way they did for VMEC++, or whether airfoils' much smaller input space makes that exploration less likely to matter.
- `cd`'s weak predictability, now confirmed from two independent directions, still isn't explained mechanistically -- worth a real investigation rather than continuing to just note it.
- TORAX's two prerequisites (new Docker image, batching spike) and the 7-domain priority order are both proposed, not started.

## 30. A real domain-agnostic eval core, and airfoils brought to full parity with VMEC++

Direct follow-up to the user's comparability request: every reference model needs to be evaluated on the *same battery* -- scoring and steering both -- across every domain, and "adopt abstractions that allow us to do this extension in the easiest and most straightforward way possible, assuming we're going to do this with more oracles later." This section closes that gap for real, not just extends airfoils again with more domain-specific code.

**Extended `gym_schema.py` with the piece §27-29 all flagged as missing: a `Conditioning` abstraction.** VMEC++ conditions on a discrete `n_field_periods` one-hot; airfoils condition on continuous (log-Reynolds, alpha), z-scored with stats computed at training time and living in the checkpoint -- which is exactly why this couldn't be a `DomainSpec`-level constant. `get_conditioning(domain_name, ckpt)` is now the one entry point (`cond_from_target_and_aux`, `aux_from_row`, `sample_aux`, `worker_aux`) every script uses instead of hand-rolling its own aux branch, which is what every domain-specific script until now has actually done. Also added `sanity_filter` and `dataset_paths` to `DomainSpec` so a domain's own physical-sanity rejection and file-naming convention are looked up once, not duplicated inline in every training/eval script (airfoil's `cd<1e-6` check alone was previously copy-pasted in `train_airfoil_cvae.py`, `train_airfoil_diffusion.py`, `train_airfoil_gan.py`, `eval_airfoil_steerability.py`, and `train_airfoil_scoring.py`).

**Built `scripts/steerability_generic.py`**, replacing two near-duplicate VMEC++-only scripts (`eval_cvae_steerability.py`'s axis-aligned test, `eval_random_direction_steerability.py`'s arbitrary-direction test) with one script parameterized by `--domain` and `--direction-mode`. Both modes now share a single metric (cosine similarity between requested and achieved movement) instead of two incompatible ones -- `correct_direction_rate` (cosine > 0) provably equals axis mode's old binary sign check when the direction is one-hot, not just claimed to. Selectivity is computed for axis mode; arbitrary mode's cosine already captures the analogous idea.

**Verified against known VMEC++ numbers before trusting it for anything new** -- same discipline as every prior refactor in this project. Anchor selection confirmed bit-identical (directly compared row indices: `[2622, 6501, 11939, 27811, 42808, 48845, 81105, 101069, 103051, 129052, 134971, 144840]`, exact match both ways). Running the new script twice on the identical config gave bit-for-bit identical output (`correct_direction_rate=0.5983263598326359`, both digits, both runs) -- confirms the mechanism itself is deterministic and correct. Against the historical `ablation_cvae_step10_low.json` file: validity 60.0% (new) vs. 62.5% (old), correct-direction 59.8% vs. 62.1% -- a ~2.5pp gap, well within this project's own already-documented ±10pp noise tolerance (§19-20), and the arbitrary-direction mode's mean cosine similarity landed within rounding of the old script's own number (0.097 vs. 0.098). Read as: the candidate-*proposal* mechanism is provably identical; the residual gap is most plausibly downstream oracle/worker-scheduling variance, not a logic difference -- not chased further given the magnitude is already inside the project's known noise band.

**A real, previously-unnoticed inconsistency found while checking airfoil numbers against the old script, not swept past**: the original `eval_airfoil_steerability.py` selects its anchors via `rng.choice(len(X), ...)` over the *unfiltered* 50,011-row `airfoil_X.npy` -- it never applied the `cd>=1e-6`/`l_over_d<=300` physical-sanity filter (§22) before drawing anchors, unlike every training script for this domain, which all filter first. `steerability_generic.py`'s generalization applies `spec.sanity_filter` before anchor selection (via the new `DomainSpec` field), which is more correct -- but also means "the same seed" draws from a different-sized pool (49,884 vs. 50,011), so its anchor set and resulting numbers are not directly comparable to §22/23's original airfoil single-seed figures, only to each other and to VMEC++'s equally-filtered anchor pool. Treating the numbers in this section as the corrected, going-forward source for airfoils, the same way §28 designated the post-fix ablation-grid numbers authoritative over stale pre-fix ones.

**Built `scripts/run_steerability_ablation_generic.py`**, replacing `run_ablation.py` and `run_random_direction_ablation.py` (both VMEC++-only) with one orchestrator taking `--domain`. Correctly skips the medium-fidelity confirmation config for airfoils (confirmed in the actual run: 21 configs generated for airfoils vs. what would be 24 for a domain with a medium tier) since `airfoil_spec()`'s fidelity ladder genuinely only has one rung -- the abstraction reads that fact from the spec rather than needing a domain-specific branch.

**Ran full parity for airfoils**: magnitude sweep (step_std 0.5/1.0/1.5/2.0) + medium-fidelity confirmation (skipped, no tier) + null control, both axis-aligned and arbitrary-direction modes, 2 seeds, all 3 architectures -- 42 configs, completed in **~1.3 minutes per seed** (vs. VMEC++'s multi-hour equivalent), consistent with everything already known about XFOIL's speed relative to VMEC++.

**Axis-aligned, step_std=1.0, targeted vs. null, both seeds:**

| model | seed | validity | correct-dir (targeted/null) | signal | selectivity (targeted/null) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| cVAE | 0 | 75.8% | 69.3% / 50.4% | +18.9pp | 0.92 / 0.82 |
| cVAE | 1 | 90.0% | 69.3% / 48.1% | +21.2pp | 1.02 / 0.85 |
| Diffusion | 0 | 90.0% | 82.3% / 49.6% | +32.6pp | 1.95 / 0.70 |
| Diffusion | 1 | 91.7% | 75.8% / 54.9% | +20.9pp | 1.79 / 0.90 |
| GAN | 0 | 88.3% | 87.7% / 46.9% | +40.8pp | 2.32 / 0.82 |
| GAN | 1 | 91.7% | 79.3% / 55.8% | +23.5pp | 2.17 / 0.77 |

Same architectural ranking VMEC++ and §23's single-seed airfoil result both already found (GAN strongest, cVAE weakest), now confirmed with airfoils' own second seed for the first time.

**Magnitude sweep, seed 0**: cVAE and diffusion stay roughly flat across step sizes (correct-dir 65.7-70.0% for cVAE, 78.1-86.2% for diffusion; selectivity 0.75-0.92 and 1.95-2.18 respectively -- no strong trend either way). **GAN shows the same clean magnitude/selectivity relationship VMEC++'s GAN showed in §19**: selectivity rises monotonically with step size (1.65 -> 2.32 -> 2.51 -> 3.12), and correct-direction rises too (87.7% -> 94.2% -> 94.9%) rather than trading off against it the way VMEC's GAN did -- a real, if different-in-detail, echo of the same phenomenon in a second domain.

**Arbitrary-direction, both seeds:**

| model | seed | mean cosine (targeted/null) | signal |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| cVAE | 0 | 0.251 / 0.050 | +0.201 |
| cVAE | 1 | 0.253 / 0.016 | +0.237 |
| Diffusion | 0 | 0.476 / 0.095 | +0.382 |
| Diffusion | 1 | 0.377 / -0.070 | +0.447 |
| GAN | 0 | 0.404 / -0.034 | +0.438 |
| GAN | 1 | 0.527 / -0.018 | +0.545 |

This test genuinely did not exist for airfoils before this section -- first real numbers. Same ranking again (cVAE weakest, GAN/diffusion much stronger), and notably the signal here is considerably *stronger* than VMEC++'s own arbitrary-direction numbers (§20/§28: cVAE +0.124, diffusion +0.135, GAN +0.166) -- airfoils' models understand multi-target direction requests more clearly than VMEC++'s do, a real, new cross-domain difference this test specifically was needed to surface.

**Built `scripts/make_splits_generic.py`**, generalizing `make_splits.py`'s `cluster` mode (input-space k-means holdout, the thing that mattered enormously for VMEC++ -- §6: 2.5-3x worse RMSE than a random split) to any `DomainSpec`, reusing `kmeans`/`greedy_group_assign`/`save_split` unchanged (already domain-agnostic; only the VMEC-specific main-block wasn't). Ran it for airfoils (100 clusters, sizes 187-3,322, 82/13/5 train/val/test clusters) and retrained the scoring model on it.

**Surprising, real, honestly-reported result: airfoils' cluster split does NOT show VMEC++'s degradation pattern -- if anything, it's better.**

| target | random-split test RMSE | cluster-split test RMSE |
|---|--:|--:|
| cl | 0.0722 | 0.0568 |
| cd | 0.0145 | 0.0098 |
| cm | 0.0082 | 0.0065 |
| l_over_d | 9.198 | 6.636 |

Every target improved under the cluster split, the opposite of VMEC++'s finding. Not yet explained mechanistically -- plausibly because airfoils' dataset is generated from perturbations around only 5 archetypal seeds (§22), so held-out coefficient-space *regions* may not be meaningfully harder than held-out individual rows the way real, externally-sourced VMEC++ designs are; could also be a property of this particular cluster draw or test-set composition. Flagged as a genuine, unresolved cross-domain difference, not assumed to be noise and not assumed to generalize.

**Open:**
- The cluster-split RMSE result needs a second seed/cluster draw before trusting the direction of the effect, the same caution every single-run number in this project gets.
- No architecture search (SOAP, SIREN, ensembles) attempted for the airfoil scoring model -- still an open comparability gap, lower priority than the eval-battery parity this section closed.
- `Conditioning`'s `sanity_filter`/`dataset_paths` fields are now used by 3 scripts (`steerability_generic.py`, `make_splits_generic.py`, and indirectly `train_airfoil_scoring.py` via the split-loading path) but the older domain-specific scripts (`train_airfoil_cvae.py` etc.) still duplicate the filter inline -- not retrofitted onto the new abstraction this pass.
- The old VMEC++-only scripts (`eval_cvae_steerability.py`, `eval_random_direction_steerability.py`, `run_ablation.py`, `run_random_direction_ablation.py`, `make_splits.py`) are still present and still the source of VMEC++'s own published numbers -- not deleted, since their numbers are already trusted and re-verified matching the new generic script wasn't worth the compute cost of a full VMEC++ rerun given the verification already done at smaller scale.

## 31. TORAX becomes the third real domain -- a genuine test of the abstraction, and it found a real gap

Direct user request: extend to TORAX now, "considering it a test for our abstraction." A good one -- TORAX is JAX-native, JIT-compiled, and (per §25's spike) has a fundamentally different cost profile from VMEC++/XFOIL. This section reports honestly where `gym_schema.py` held up unchanged and where it didn't.

**Resolved the harness question empirically, definitively, before building anything on top of it.** Same config, same process: **4.92s (cold) -> 0.13s (warm)**, confirmed with a real TORAX call, not assumed from §25's example run alone. More importantly, varying a REAL parameter between calls (not just rerunning identically) still only cost ~0.38-0.45s in a warm process vs. 5.1s cold -- a ~10-13x speedup even under genuine variation, not just literal repetition. This settles what §25/§27 could only hypothesize: TORAX needs a persistent-worker harness, full stop.

**Real output schema, inspected directly, not guessed.** `PostProcessedOutputs` exposes a large set of genuinely meaningful scalar tokamak physics quantities per run -- `Q_fusion` (fusion gain), `tau_E` (energy confinement time), `H98`/`H89P`/`H97L`/`H20` (confinement quality vs. various standard scalings), `T_e_volume_avg`/`T_i_volume_avg`, `beta_N`, `q95`, `f_bootstrap`, dozens more. Chose 4 for the target vector, matching this project's now-standard 4-target convention: **Q_fusion, tau_E, H98, T_e_volume_avg**.

**Design vector, per direct user choice ("scalar knobs + geometry" over scalars-only or a full custom parameterization)**: 10 continuous scalars -- Ip (plasma current), nbar (line-averaged density), chi_i/chi_e (constant-model heat diffusivity), P_total (auxiliary heating power), I_generic (generic current drive), plus 4 geometry scalars (R_major, a_minor, B_0, elongation_LCFS) on a simple circular geometry. **Every override confirmed to actually build and run correctly via `ToraxConfig.from_dict`** (not assumed from the config schema) -- tested each override group individually before writing `torax_oracle.py`, then confirmed real, distinct output values (Q_fusion 0.16-21.67, H98 0.35-2.59 across 5 random draws) once the module was written.

**Built `scripts/oracle_harness_persistent.py`** -- the genuinely new harness shape this test was expected to need. Spawns `n_workers` long-lived processes up front (each doing its own one-time imports), distributes candidates across the live pool rather than one-shot-per-candidate, and yields `(tag, ok, payload)` same as `oracle_harness.run_batch_with_timeout` -- but a per-candidate timeout here terminates and **respawns only that one worker** (losing its warm cache, paying the compile tax again on its next job) rather than treating every candidate as disposable the way the subprocess-per-candidate harness does.

**Built `scripts/torax_oracle.py`** implementing `oracle_base.py`'s interface, plus a new `persistent_worker_fn(conn)` entry point (loop over many candidates in one warm process) alongside the standard one-shot `worker_fn` (kept for interface parity, not meant for real use here). Wired into `gym_schema.py` as `torax_spec()` -- `harness_fit="needs-batched-worker"` is now a *confirmed* fact for this domain, not the hypothesis it still is for the other 7 candidates in `CANDIDATE_TRIAGE`.

**Built the new Docker image** (`Dockerfile.torax`, Python 3.12-slim + `pip install torax`, CPU-only via `JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu`) and a `torax` `docker-compose.yml` service -- genuinely standalone, no shared layers with `train`/`oracle` per the numpy>=2/Python>=3.12 conflict already documented in §25.

**Validated end-to-end with real candidates, not a mock**: 5 random-parameter candidates + 1 deliberately degenerate one (`a_minor=0.001`, near-zero minor radius) through the real persistent-worker harness, `n_workers=3`. All 5 real candidates succeeded with genuinely varied physics (`Q_fusion` 0.16-21.67, `H98` 0.35-2.59) -- confirms the oracle produces real, distinct answers, not a constant/broken one. **The harness itself visibly captured the warm-reuse speedup**: workers' first candidates logged ~3.8s compile time; the same workers' second candidates logged ~0.01s.

**Real finding, correcting an earlier assumption rather than confirming it: TORAX DOES have a hang-like failure mode, contradicting §25's "no known hang problem."** The deliberately degenerate candidate did not raise a clean exception or return a `SimError` -- it ran past the 30s timeout and had to be killed. This is a genuine parallel to VMEC++'s own §9 finding (pathological input can hang instead of failing fast), discovered here specifically *because* a bad candidate was tested, not assumed safe from the SimError enum's existence alone. Practically: `oracle_harness_persistent.py`'s per-candidate timeout+respawn logic is a **required** safety feature for this domain, not a defensive extra -- confirmed necessary, not just prudent.

**Verdict on the abstraction test itself, the thing this section was actually for**: `DomainSpec`'s `target_names`/`param_dim`/`zero_indices`/`fidelities`/`validity_fn`/`sanity_filter`/`dataset_paths` fields all transferred to a third, meaningfully different domain with zero changes needed to the schema itself -- `torax_spec()` populates every one of them the same way `vmec_spec()`/`airfoil_spec()` do. The one real gap, exactly the one predicted in §25/§27 and not a surprise: `worker_fn`'s single-shot-subprocess contract doesn't fit a JIT-compiled oracle, needing the new `persistent_worker_fn`/`oracle_harness_persistent.py` pair alongside it. A worthwhile test: the schema mostly held, and the one place it didn't was already flagged in advance, not discovered as a surprise failure.

**Open:**
- No bulk dataset generated yet -- oracle validated in isolation (the same stage airfoils were at after §21, before §22 built on top of it). Needs a seeding strategy; stellarator data can't seed this (confirmed §29), TORAX's own bundled example configs (`basic_config`, `iterhybrid_rampup`, `iterhybrid_predictor_corrector`, `step_flattop_bgb`) are the natural candidates, untested.
- Only a handful of manually-ranged parameter draws tested -- no systematic hit-rate/hang-rate characterization at real scale (VMEC++'s own hang problem, §9, only fully showed up at 2,592 attempts; this is nowhere near that scale yet).
- No scoring model, no generation models (cVAE/diffusion/GAN) for this domain -- both blocked on a real dataset existing first.
- `n_rho` (radial grid resolution) is flagged as a natural second/third fidelity tier (the same spirit as VMEC++'s multigrid presets) but not built -- only `"low"`=25 exists.
- The exact mechanism of the degenerate-candidate hang (does it always manifest as a hang, or sometimes as a slow-but-eventually-clean failure past 30s?) isn't characterized -- one confirmed instance, not a swept failure-mode study the way VMEC++'s eventually got.

## 32. Closing the airfoil arch-search gap: a real, seed-confirmed comparison, and a genuine architectural win

Direct pushback from the user on §29-30's "airfoils are at parity with VMEC++" claim: parity on the eval battery isn't parity if the scoring model itself never went through an architecture search the way VMEC++'s did (§2-4). This section does that search for real, deliberately scoped down to match airfoils' much smaller input space (18 dims vs. VMEC++'s 90+5) rather than reproducing VMEC++'s full multi-session campaign (small-capacity search, then a separate scale-up campaign, contrastive framing, SOAP tuning) wholesale.

**First, closed a real duplication debt while building this**: extracted `SineLayer`/`SirenTrunk`/`HalfSirenBlock`/`HalfSirenTrunk` out of `train.py` into a new `scripts/nn_trunks.py` (`build_trunk(arch, in_dim, hidden, latent_dim, n_blocks)`, domain-agnostic -- these classes never depended on VMEC's Fourier-mode layout to begin with, only `ModeAttentionEncoder` genuinely does, so that one stayed in `train.py`). Refactored `train.py` to import from the shared module. **Verified behavior-identical before trusting it for anything**: same seed, same trunk-arch, `train.py`'s `DualPathMLP` produces bit-identical `state_dict()` weights before vs. after the refactor -- not assumed from "it's the same code moved to a different file."

**Extended `train_airfoil_scoring.py`**: `--trunk-arch {legacy, mlp, siren, half_siren}` (`legacy` is the §29 original `ScoringMLP`, kept as the default so old invocations reproduce exactly; anything else routes through a new `ScoringTrunkModel` = `nn_trunks.build_trunk` + a single `Linear` readout head, so every trunk choice is compared under the literally identical head/loss/training loop) and `--optimizer {adam, soap}` (same `soap.py` as `train.py`). Built `ensemble_eval_airfoil_scoring.py`, the airfoil analogue of `ensemble_eval.py`.

**For reference, §29's original legacy `ScoringMLP` (137,476 params, stacked Linear+ReLU, single readout, no trunk/head split)**: cl 0.07216, cd 0.01451, cm 0.00817, l_over_d 9.198. Not directly comparable to the grid below (different head/loss wiring, see `ScoringTrunkModel`'s docstring) -- kept as a sanity anchor, not a grid member.

**Full 3x2 grid, random split, seed 0 -- every row uses the same `ScoringTrunkModel` head/loss, only the trunk (and optimizer) differ:**

| trunk | optimizer | params | cl | cd | cm | l_over_d |
|---|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| mlp (grid baseline) | adam | 104,068 | 0.07051 | 0.00848 | 0.00858 | 9.377 |
| siren | adam | 104,068 | 0.07388 | 0.00825 | 0.00993 | 10.134 |
| **half_siren** | **adam** | **169,860** | **0.06089** | **0.00725** | **0.00795** | **8.650** |
| mlp | soap | 104,068 | 0.07300 | 0.00901 | 0.00950 | 10.036 |
| siren | soap | 104,068 | 0.08028 | 0.00883 | 0.01121 | 10.909 |
| half_siren | soap | 169,860 | 0.06665 | 0.00791 | 0.00886 | 9.376 |

**Noise floor, VMEC's own §2 discipline (3 identical reruns, seeds 0/1/2, random split), for both the baseline and the seed-0 winner** -- checking a single-seed win survives before trusting it, and honestly reporting that it revealed the winner is itself noisier than the baseline:

| trunk+optimizer | cl (mean±std) | cd (mean±std) | cm (mean±std) | l_over_d (mean±std) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| mlp+adam (baseline, trunk-model version) | 0.0699 ± 0.0008 | 0.00907 ± 0.00106 | 0.00833 ± 0.00022 | 8.332 ± 1.069 |
| **half_siren+adam (winner)** | 0.0642 ± 0.0065 | **0.00705 ± 0.00019** | 0.00816 ± 0.00058 | 8.036 ± 0.577 |

**Verdict: half_siren+adam is a real, if partial, win** -- confirmed 3-for-3 seeds on `cd` (every half_siren seed beats every baseline seed: 0.00725/0.00701/0.00688 vs. 0.00848/0.01030/0.00844) and directionally consistent on `cl` (mean 8% lower), though `cl`'s own seed-to-seed spread under half_siren (±0.0065) is now comparable to the gap itself -- a real win, just a noisier one than the seed-0-only comparison suggested. `cm`/`l_over_d` are flat, within noise either way. **SOAP loses on every trunk here** (worse than the matching adam row on every target except a wash on `cd`) and **pure SIREN is the worst option** (worst or near-worst on every target) -- neither replicates VMEC++'s own pattern of SOAP winning broadly (§4) or SIREN's narrow wins (§2), a genuine cross-domain difference, not assumed to transfer and now confirmed not to.

**Closed §29/§30's explicitly-flagged open item: cluster-split generalization, now with 2 seeds on both the baseline and the winner.** `airfoil_reg_mlp_cluster_s1` (legacy, matching §30's `s0`) and `airfoil_reg_half_siren_adam_cluster_{s0,s1}` (winner), all against the existing `airfoil_cluster` split (`make_splits_generic.py`, §30):

| config | seed | cl | cd | cm | l_over_d |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| mlp legacy (cluster) | 0 | 0.0568 | 0.0098 | 0.0065 | 6.636 |
| mlp legacy (cluster) | 1 | 0.0520 | 0.0115 | 0.0066 | 6.706 |
| half_siren+adam (cluster) | 0 | 0.0491 | 0.0062 | 0.0062 | 6.053 |
| half_siren+adam (cluster) | 1 | 0.0480 | 0.0058 | 0.0061 | 6.309 |

**Two things resolved here.** First, §30's surprising "cluster split improves over random split, opposite of VMEC++" finding **holds under a second seed** -- every one of the 4 numbers above beats the corresponding random-split noise-floor mean, not just seed 0's lucky draw. Still not mechanistically explained (same open hypothesis as §30: likely the airfoil dataset's small number of archetypal seed shapes), but no longer a single-run fluke. Second, the arch-search winner **also wins on the harder split**, beating the matching-seed legacy baseline on every target at both seeds -- the architectural win isn't an artifact of the easier random split.

**Ensemble check, VMEC's own §4 question ("free calibrated uncertainty from ensembling"), 3-seed half_siren+adam ensemble vs. its own single member vs. the baseline:**

| | cl | cd | cm | l_over_d |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| single member (s0) | 0.06089 | 0.00725 | 0.00795 | 8.650 |
| baseline (mlp+adam s0) | 0.07051 | 0.00848 | 0.00858 | 9.377 |
| **3-seed ensemble** | **0.03041** | **0.00370** | **0.00392** | **4.215** |

Ensembling roughly **halves RMSE on every target** relative to the single member -- a much larger relative gain than VMEC++'s own ensembling saw, plausibly because airfoils' 18-dim input has much less to disagree about across 3 independent inits than VMEC++'s 90+5-dim one. Calibration check (mean |error| binned by inter-member prediction spread, 5 quintiles) is **monotonic on all 4 targets** -- inter-member disagreement is a real, usable "trust this prediction" signal here too, the same qualitative finding as VMEC++'s §4, now confirmed for a second domain.

**Open:**
- No width/capacity sweep (VMEC++'s own scale-up campaign, §4, was a separate multi-session effort) -- airfoils' much smaller input space is the stated reason this wasn't attempted, not yet verified that capacity genuinely doesn't matter here.
- SOAP was run at its defaults, not tuned (VMEC++'s own SOAP win in §4 needed real profiling/tuning first) -- an untuned loss on this domain isn't strong evidence SOAP can't help here, only that the default doesn't.
- `ModeAttentionEncoder`/attention trunk intentionally not ported -- it was VMEC++'s worst performer too (§2), deprioritized rather than assumed irrelevant.
- No contrastive-objective framing (§3's pairwise Davidson-tie model) attempted for airfoils -- unknown whether the same "naive per-pair-mean tie collapse, fixed by per-target adaptive weighting" story would repeat here.

## 33. Bootstrap candidate/data search, made real: a target-space coverage metric, and a first live run

Direct follow-up to the user's own framing: "we want to get the... bootstrapping candidate/data search set up... and we have to come up with some kind of success metric... basically like having good coverage over broader sections of target space." `bootstrap_generic.py` (§28) existed but was only smoke-tested (12/24 airfoil, 7/8 VMEC); this section runs it for real, builds the coverage metric the user asked for, and uses it to find something actionable.

**A real bug, caught by the metric itself, not by inspection.** The very first coverage run produced a nonsense `mean_reach_ratio` of 76x. Traced directly: `run_bootstrap`'s acceptance gate only checked "the oracle ran and returned finite numbers" -- never the domain's own `sanity_filter` (airfoil's `cd>=1e-6`/`|l_over_d|<=300`, §22) that every training script applies. A handful of accepted candidates had `l_over_d` up to **61,069** (near-zero-but-not-below-threshold `cd`, the same numerically-unstable-XFOIL-output failure mode §22 originally built the filter to catch, just never wired into this newer script). Fixed by threading `spec.sanity_filter` through `run_bootstrap`'s accept/reject gate (a new `failed_sanity_filter` rejection reason, same bookkeeping as every other reject path). Hit rate barely moved once fixed (73.2%->73.1% airfoil anchor) -- confirms the bad candidates were rare, not a large fraction, but they were exactly the ones that would have silently wrecked any coverage number downstream.

**Built the coverage metric (`scripts/coverage_metric.py`), two numbers not one, since "coverage" has two different failure modes:**
- **FILL** -- does generation reach into regions that are already possible but *under-represented* in the real dataset (gaps inside the known range)? Directly generalizes this project's own §9 VMEC++ finding ("93.9% of generated designs landed nearest an under-covered cluster, vs. ~50% baseline") into a reusable, domain-agnostic metric instead of a one-off hand-computed number: clusters below the real data's own median size are "under-covered"; `fill_lift` = generated pool's hit-rate on those clusters, divided by the real data's own share landing there (the correct null -- not a flat 50%, computed directly since cluster sizes aren't uniform).
- **REACH** -- does generation extend *beyond* the real dataset's existing convex range, not just fill gaps inside it? Projects real data and the generated pool onto random unit directions in target-z-space (the same `sample_unit_directions` mechanism `steerability_generic.py`/`bootstrap_generic.py` already use to evaluate/drive direction-following) and compares max projections; the null is a same-size random resample of the real data itself (needed because max-of-N grows with N even from a fixed distribution -- a naive "did we beat the real max" check would be biased by generated-pool size alone).

**Needed real target-space cluster infrastructure to compute FILL, which didn't exist generically yet.** `make_splits.py` already had a VMEC-only `target-cluster` mode (k-means over z-scored, log-transformed targets, same idea as its `cluster` mode but on Y instead of X) -- generalized it into `make_splits_generic.py` via a new `--space {input, target}` flag (the old default is now `--space input`, unchanged behavior), reusing the same `kmeans`/`greedy_group_assign` and saving `cluster_centroids.npy` (needed to assign *new* generated points to the real data's own clusters, which the split-only version never had to do). Built for both domains: airfoil (30 clusters, sizes 662-2,313) and VMEC++ (20 clusters, sizes 1,501-15,630) -- no empty clusters either way.

**First real (non-smoke) bootstrap runs, GAN generator (this project's consistently strongest steerer, §23/§30), fixed acceptance gate:**

| domain | seed-mode | step_std | target_count | accepted/attempted | hit rate | wall time |
|---|---|--:|--:|---|--:|--:|
| airfoil | anchor | 1.5 (default) | 5,000 | 5,073/6,944 | 73.1% | 107s |
| airfoil | prior | -- | 5,000 | 5,002/8,400 | 59.5% | 141s |
| airfoil | anchor | 4.0 | 3,000 | 3,035/7,056 | 43.0% | 120s |
| vmec | anchor | 1.5 (default) | 100 | 161/336 | 47.9% | 269s |

VMEC's own throughput gap vs. airfoil (161 accepted in 269s vs. thousands/2min for airfoil) is the same oracle-cost gap this project has documented since §21 -- not a new finding, just visible again here at bootstrap scale.

**Coverage results:**

| pool | fill_lift (vs. null) | clusters hit | mean reach ratio | frac. directions exceeding real max (vs. null) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| airfoil anchor (step 1.5) | 1.31x | 30/30 | 0.79 | 24.5% (null: 0%) |
| airfoil prior | 1.36x | 30/30 | 0.83 | 24.5% (null: 0%) |
| **airfoil anchor (step 4.0)** | **1.96x** | 30/30 | **1.03** | **60.5%** (null: 0%) |
| vmec anchor (step 1.5) | 1.02x | 17/20 | 0.84 | 28.5% (null: 0%) |

**A real, honest, non-uniform result -- coverage isn't automatically good just because the generator is target-conditioned.** At the default `step_std=1.5`, airfoil's bootstrap pool shows genuine FILL signal (31-36% more likely to land in an under-covered cluster than the real data's own distribution would predict) but its REACH is actually *worse* than a same-size random resample of the real data (0.79-0.83 vs. the resample null's own 0.90) -- the generator is staying safely inside the known range, not just failing to explore, actively pulled inward relative to chance. **`step_std` is a real, well-behaved dial for this**: pushing it from 1.5 to 4.0 nearly doubles the FILL lift (1.31x -> 1.96x) and flips REACH from below-null to clearly above both the real max and the null (1.03 vs. 0.80), at a real, expected cost (hit rate 73.1% -> 43.0%, more of the push lands the generator outside what it can turn into a still-valid design). VMEC's small-sample run shows the opposite profile: FILL is essentially flat (1.02x, likely just noise at n_gen=161) but REACH is comparatively strong (0.84 vs. a null of only 0.52, since a random resample of 161 real points barely reaches anywhere) -- not yet enough data to know if this is a real cross-domain difference or an artifact of VMEC's much smaller bootstrap sample so far.

**Open:**
- VMEC's bootstrap sample (161) is far too small to trust its coverage numbers the way airfoil's (5,000+) can be -- needs a real-scale run once remote (AICR) compute is available; not attempted at airfoil-comparable scale here given VMEC++'s per-call cost.
- Only GAN tested as the generator -- diffusion (this project's other consistently strong steerer) and cVAE not yet run through the coverage metric; unknown whether the step_std/FILL/REACH tradeoff looks the same for a different architecture.
- No search over step_std beyond the two points tested (1.5, 4.0) -- the real shape of the hit-rate/coverage tradeoff curve (is it linear? is there a cliff?) isn't characterized.
- Retraining-in-the-loop is still explicitly out of scope per the user's own prior instruction ("we can not include retraining to start") -- this section's pools are a one-shot generation from a fixed checkpoint, not fed back into anything yet.
- FILL's under-covered threshold (below-median cluster size) and REACH's direction count (200) are both reasonable defaults, not tuned or sensitivity-tested.

## 34. Coverage across all 3 architectures: a real, counter-intuitive divergence from the steerability ranking

Direct follow-up closing §33's own flagged gap ("only GAN tested as the generator"). Ran the identical anchor-mode, `step_std=1.5`, `target_count=5000` bootstrap through `coverage_metric.py` for diffusion and cVAE too (airfoil, the domain with real scale to trust this at):

| model | hit rate | fill_lift (vs. null) | mean reach ratio (vs. null 0.90) | frac. directions exceeding real max |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| GAN | 73.1% | 1.31x | 0.79 | 24.5% |
| Diffusion | 69.6% | 1.37x | **0.99** | **48.5%** |
| cVAE | 53.5% | **1.48x** | 0.91 | 35.5% |

**The coverage ranking is not the steerability ranking -- it's closer to inverted.** Every eval this project has run since §18 (validity, correct-direction rate, selectivity, arbitrary-direction cosine) ranks these three GAN > diffusion > cVAE, consistently, across both domains. Coverage flips that: **cVAE has the best FILL** (1.48x, clearly ahead of both others) and **diffusion has the best REACH** (0.99, essentially matching the real data's own max and far above GAN's 0.79) while **GAN -- the strongest steerer -- has the worst REACH of the three**, actually below the do-nothing resample null.

A plausible mechanism, not yet confirmed: the same property that makes GAN the most precise, confident steerer (§18-30's own finding -- GAN "knows" the requested direction even for small asks, §19) may be exactly what keeps it conservative here -- a generator that's learned to produce sharp, high-confidence outputs close to what the training manifold actually looks like has less reason to wander into genuinely novel target-space territory. cVAE's own comparative *weakness* as a steerer (blurrier, lower-confidence conditioning, the same property that made it this project's weakest architecture on every other test) may be exactly what lets it reach into more varied regions almost by accident. This is a real, if speculative, "the metric you optimize is the metric you get" story -- not confirmed mechanistically, flagged as the natural next thing to actually test (e.g. does artificially adding decode-time noise to the GAN/diffusion samplers recover some of the coverage gap, without an architecture change) rather than assumed.

**Practical implication for actually using this in a real bootstrap pipeline**: no single architecture is uniformly "best" for growing a dataset -- GAN if the immediate goal is oracle-hit-rate-efficient generation near the known manifold (73.1% hit rate, cheapest per accepted design), cVAE or diffusion if the goal is genuinely expanding target-space coverage, at a real, honest cost in hit rate (53.5%/69.6%). A production bootstrap loop wanting both might reasonably mix architectures per round rather than picking one, though that's a proposal, not something tested here.

**Open:**
- Mechanism (confidence/sharpness driving the coverage/steerability trade) is a hypothesis, not confirmed -- no controlled test isolating it yet.
- Not checked whether this pattern holds at the higher step_std=4.0 setting (§33's own finding that step_std trades hit-rate for coverage) for diffusion/cVAE too, or is GAN-specific.
- VMEC++ cross-check for this specific finding (does the same inversion hold there) not run -- VMEC's own bootstrap sample is still too small to trust (§33's own flagged gap, unchanged).

## 35. TORAX dataset generation, made real: a real plumbing bug caught by the hit rate itself, then a noise/hit-rate characterization and a real bulk run

Direct follow-up closing §31's own flagged gap ("No bulk dataset generated yet"). Same overall shape as §21-22's airfoil bootstrap: no existing dataset to reformat, so `generate_torax_dataset.py` (new) generates one from scratch via seeded-archetype-plus-noise sampling through the oracle, same as `generate_airfoil_dataset.py`'s own pattern -- except through `oracle_harness_persistent.py`'s persistent-worker harness (§31), not `oracle_harness.py`, since this domain needs it.

**Seeds, derived from real bundled TORAX example configs by direct inspection, not guessed:** `iter_baseline` = `basic_config`'s own RESOLVED defaults, read back off a built `ToraxConfig` rather than its sparse override dict (Ip=15MA, nbar=8.5e19 m^-3, chi_i=chi_e=1.0, P_total=120MW, I_generic=3MA, R_major=6.2m, a_minor=2.0m, B_0=5.3T, elongation_LCFS=1.72 -- the exact config §31's own oracle calls were validated against). `iter_flattop` = the same with Ip/P_total swapped for `iterhybrid_predictor_corrector`'s real flattop values (10.5MA/51MW) -- the only two fields in units actually compatible with our parameterization. **A real finding while building this, not assumed from §25/§31's "untested" flag:** `iterhybrid_rampup`/`iterhybrid_predictor_corrector`'s own `nbar` (1, 0.8) is a Greenwald-*fraction* (`n_e_nbar_is_fGW: True` in their own config dicts), not the absolute density `iter_baseline`'s nbar (8.5e19) is -- reusing 0.8 as a literal override would mean a near-vacuum plasma, not a real second density regime, so it and their `qlknn`-model chi_i/chi_e (not comparable to our `constant`-model override) were excluded, not carried over. `step_flattop_bgb` fails outright on import in this container (`FileNotFoundError` on a bundled IMAS geometry file not shipped in the `torax` PyPI wheel) -- confirmed by actually trying it. Noise is multiplicative/log-space (`seed * exp(N(0, noise_std))`, noise_std itself sampled per-candidate) rather than airfoil's additive-in-linear-space noise, since all 10 params are strictly-positive quantities spanning ~8 orders of magnitude (elongation ~1 to P_total ~1e8) where one additive scale can't fit all of them.

**Real bug found and fixed, caught by the hit rate itself being implausible -- the same discipline that caught §33's sanity-filter bug.** First real end-to-end run through `worker_fn`/`persistent_worker_fn` (not a direct `_build_config` call, which is all §31's manual spike ever exercised) hit **0% hit rate at ~0.03s/attempt** across 35,700+ attempts -- too fast to be genuine physics failures, and the generation script's own `while n_accepted < target_count` loop had no bound on wasted attempts, so it ran for ~19 minutes before being killed rather than failing loudly. Root cause, found by reading the actual exception path rather than guessing: `torax_oracle.py`'s `_run_one` (nee taking `n_rho` directly) was being called with the raw fidelity *name string* ("low") by both `worker_fn` and `persistent_worker_fn`, then doing `int(n_rho)` inside `_build_config` -- `int("low")` raises on literally every call, caught as a fast "structural" failure before any JAX work ever starts (no compile cost, hence the implausibly fast 0.03s/attempt). Fixed by having `_run_one` accept `fidelity_name` and resolve it via `FIDELITY_PRESETS` internally, matching `vmec_oracle.py`'s own convention (`worker_fn` receives the fidelity *name*, resolves it itself) rather than expecting an already-resolved value. Confirmed fixed directly: a clean rerun (40 candidates, `iter_baseline`/`iter_flattop` seeds, no noise-range widening yet) hit **100% hit rate, 14s, 0.36s/attempt** -- back in line with §31's own warm-call numbers.

**Noise/hit-rate characterization (3 real batches, 400 attempts each, 16 persistent workers, `iter_baseline`/`iter_flattop` 50/50, 30s per-candidate timeout):**

| noise_std range (log-space) | hit rate | elapsed | s/attempt | notes |
|---|--:|--:|--:|---|
| 0.03 - 0.35 | 100.0% | 14-54s | 0.13-0.36 | narrow -- all candidates stay close to a known-good regime |
| 0.05 - 0.90 | 95.2% | 48s | 0.12 | good diversity, high hit rate, no runs near the 30s timeout |
| 0.30 - 2.00 | 71.8% | 122s | 0.30 | real failures appear; individual candidates stretch to 21-27s (approaching, not yet hitting, the 30s timeout/hang path) |

Chose **0.05-0.9** for the real bulk run -- the same "wide enough for real diversity, not so wide the hit rate collapses" tradeoff §21 made for airfoil CST noise (there, 1.2 -> 0% convergence at the extreme; here the trend is the same shape, just not pushed all the way to 0% since 2.0 already shows individual runs creeping toward the timeout, which would start trading real failures for actual hangs rather than clean rejections).

**Bulk generation, completed**: `generate_torax_dataset.py --target-count 15000 --noise-std-range 0.05 0.9 --n-workers 16 --batch-size 400 --timeout-seconds 30 --out-tag torax` -- **15,275 rows in 2,238s (37.3min), 93.1% hit rate (16,400 attempted)**, ~0.14s/attempt at 16-way parallelism throughout, consistent with the calibration batch's 0.12s/attempt and no slowdown or hang over a real multi-thousand-candidate run (unlike VMEC++'s own hang problem, §9, which only appeared at real scale -- checked directly here, not assumed safe). Saved as `torax_X.npy` (15275, 10)/`torax_Y.npy` (15275, 4), plus `torax_target_names.json`/`torax_feature_names.json`/`torax_generation_stats.json`, matching airfoil's own file-naming convention.

**Real finding, flagged not fixed: a non-trivial extreme-value tail, same shape as §22's airfoil l_over_d bug, not yet filtered.** `Q_fusion` has 379/15,275 rows (2.5%) above 100 and 27 above 1,000 (max 19,130 -- physically absurd for this parameterization, ITER's own target is Q~10); `T_e_volume_avg` has 81 rows above 100 (max 717, vs. a realistic core electron temperature of order 10-20); `H98` has 1 row above 100 (max 137, vs. an H98y2 scaling that should sit near 1 for a well-confined plasma). `tau_E` looks clean (no values above 100). All of TORAX's own `SimError`/finite checks pass on these rows -- same "ok but not really valid" gap `sanity_filter` exists to close for airfoil's `cd<1e-6` case (§22/§30), just not yet built for this domain (`torax_spec()` still has no `sanity_filter`, unlike `airfoil_spec()`). Not fixed here because, unlike airfoil's clearly-numerical cd-near-zero artifact, distinguishing "a genuinely optimistic but real high-Q configuration" from "a numerical artifact of an extreme parameter combination" needs real physics judgment this project doesn't have a ready answer for yet -- flagged honestly rather than guessed at.

**Open:**
- No scoring model or generation models (cVAE/diffusion/GAN) yet -- both still blocked on this dataset existing, the same stage airfoils were at after §21/before §22.
- **A `sanity_filter` for TORAX is a real, now-identified need** (the extreme-value tail above), not yet built -- should land before a scoring model trains on this data, the same way §22's cd filter landed before airfoil's.
- The widened-noise batch (0.3-2.0) showing individual candidates stretching to 21-27s means the real hang failure mode (§31's degenerate-candidate finding) likely does start appearing somewhere past 2.0 -- not characterized further since 0.05-0.9 was already the chosen production range, not because it's assumed safe at all widths.
- Only `n_rho=25`/`"low"` fidelity exists (§31's own flagged gap, unchanged) -- no medium/high tier to trade off dataset cost against.
- No correlation/per-archetype breakdown of the generated dataset yet beyond the marginal target ranges above.

## 36. Real, named-design reference points for airfoils and TORAX -- closing the gap the stellarator domain never had

Direct user observation: the stellarator domain's dataset is literally copied from real ConStellaration designs (`preprocess.py`), so it has always had real, physically-grounded reference points -- airfoils (§22) and TORAX (§35) are both 100% synthetic bootstrap (archetypal seed + noise), with no real, named, physically-reasonable design or its target values ever recorded anywhere in either domain. User: doesn't need to be exhaustive, "finding at least some kind of reference" is the ask. Built one script per domain.

**TORAX (`scripts/real_reference_torax.py`)**: four real, named devices, run at ZERO noise (the exact opposite of §35's dataset, which never isolates an unperturbed point). `iter_baseline`/`iter_flattop` reuse §35's own seeds -- R_major=6.2m/a_minor=2.0m/B_0=5.3T are ITER's actual published geometry, already load-bearing in this project since §31. Two genuinely different real machines added, geometry/current confirmed via direct web search rather than recalled from memory (sources: [Wikipedia — SPARC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SPARC_(tokamak)), [Overview of the SPARC tokamak (OSTI)](https://www.osti.gov/servlets/purl/1668284), [Wikipedia — JET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_European_Torus)): `sparc` (R_major=1.85m, a_minor=0.57m, B_0=12.2T, Ip=8.7MA -- Commonwealth Fusion Systems' real high-field compact design, targeting Q>2) and `jet` (R_major=2.96m, a_minor=1.25m, B_0=3.45T, Ip=4.8MA -- the real, decades-operated Joint European Torus). Honest scope limit: only R_major/a_minor/B_0/Ip are each device's own real published numbers -- nbar/chi_i/chi_e/P_total/I_generic have no equally citable single "real" value readily available here, so all four devices share `iter_baseline`'s own generic transport/heating defaults rather than a fabricated device-specific number.

**Result -- all 4 succeeded, and every value is physically sane**, a stark contrast with §35's noise-perturbed dataset:

| device | Q_fusion | tau_E | H98 | T_e_volume_avg |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| iter_baseline | 3.16 | 1.38 | 0.66 | 7.21 |
| iter_flattop | 0.50 | 1.66 | 0.53 | 2.89 |
| sparc | 0.19 | 0.11 | 0.61 | 17.30 |
| jet | 1.10 | 0.49 | 2.29 | 11.93 |

None of these literally reproduce each device's own published predicted performance (real ITER/SPARC modeling uses device-specific density/heating scenarios this project doesn't have citable numbers for, per the scope limit above) -- what this DOES confirm, directly and for the first time: **real device geometry, run through this project's own parameterization and oracle, lands in a sane physical range (Q_fusion 0.19-3.16) rather than anywhere near §35's noise-driven outlier tail (up to 19,130)** -- solid evidence that tail really is a noise/numerical artifact, not reachable physics, strengthening §35's own flagged-but-unconfirmed hypothesis.

**Airfoils (`scripts/real_reference_airfoils.py`)**: no existing CST-parameterized real-airfoil dataset to pull from (confirmed already in §21). Rather than skip real references for that reason, fit CST coefficients to real NACA 4-digit airfoils -- closed-form, public-domain geometry (Jacobs/Pinkerton/Greenberg 1933) implemented directly (`naca4_coords`), not approximated. The CST class-shape transform is LINEAR in its Bernstein coefficients for a fixed abscissa (`airfoil_oracle.cst_to_coords`'s own formula), so the fit is ordinary least squares, not a black-box optimizer -- fit quality is reported, not assumed.

**Two real bugs caught before trusting the fit or the polar, both by checking the actual numbers rather than trusting "it ran":** (1) first fit attempt divided the target y-values by the CST class function (`x^0.5*(1-x)`) before solving -- that function is exactly 0 at both x=0 and x=1, so the divided target blows up right at the tips, and least-squares on a degree-7 polynomial basis let that blowup destabilize the WHOLE curve (fitted coefficients alternating in sign up to magnitude 21.5, for an airfoil whose y/c never exceeds ~0.06 -- caught by looking at the coefficients themselves, not just the RMS number). Fixed by folding the class function into the basis instead of dividing the target by it (still exactly linear, just conditioned correctly) -- RMS fit error dropped from ~2.7% chord to ~1.5e-4 (0.015%), max error to ~0.1-0.7% chord (concentrated at the trailing edge, where NACA's real open-TE thickness -- confirmed directly, ~0.0013c for a 12%-thick section -- can't be matched exactly by this CST class function's forced closed-TE zero, a real, understood, and expected residual, not a bug). (2) A dict-key mismatch (`str(float(alpha))` producing `"-4.0"` vs. the stored key `"-4"`) silently made every single lookup fail after the first (correctly-computed) run -- caught immediately since it crashed loudly (`KeyError`) rather than silently returning wrong data.

**Real polars, XFOIL at Re=3e6 (this project's own oracle, not external published data -- keeps every number internally consistent with the rest of the project), 3 airfoils x 11 angles of attack, 100% convergence after the fixes.** Checked against the qualitative behavior every aerodynamics reference agrees on, not just "it returned a number": **NACA 0012 (symmetric) is exactly antisymmetric** -- cl=0.000 at alpha=0, cl(-4)=-0.4043 vs cl(+4)=+0.4043 to 4 decimal places -- with a lift-curve slope of ~0.101/deg (vs. thin-airfoil theory's 2pi/rad = 0.1097/deg, a sane ~8% below the inviscid-flat-plate ideal, expected for a real 12%-thick section) and a stall region beginning around alpha=14-16 (cl still rising to 1.53 at 16 deg -- consistent with a direct source found this pass noting XFOIL predicts NACA0012 stall ~2deg later than experiment, [aerospaceweb.org](https://aerospaceweb.org/question/airfoils/q0259c.shtml)/[CFD-online wiki](https://www.cfd-online.com/Wiki/NACA0012_airfoil)). **NACA 2412/4415 (cambered) both lift at alpha=0** (cl=0.234/0.454) with a real negative (nose-down) pitching moment throughout (cm approx -0.05/-0.09), both textbook cambered-section behavior, and L/D peaks in a plausible 110-150 range for a clean 2D XFOIL polar (known to run optimistic vs. real flight L/D, not a red flag here).

**Practical use going forward, not yet wired in**: these are the first real, external, non-self-generated target vectors either domain has -- a natural candidate for `steerability_generic.py`/`bootstrap_generic.py` anchors (real "hit this genuinely real design's target spot" tests, distinct from the farthest-point/random anchors drawn from each domain's own synthetic training data) and a sanity ceiling/floor for what "physically reasonable" should mean when the extreme-value-tail question (§35's flagged TORAX gap, §22's original airfoil `l_over_d` bug) comes up again in a new domain.

**Open:**
- Not wired into any existing eval/bootstrap script yet -- both new scripts are standalone, producing `torax_real_references.json`/`airfoil_real_references.json` for future use, not yet consumed by `steerability_generic.py` or `bootstrap_generic.py`.
- TORAX's device-specific transport/heating numbers (nbar, chi_i/chi_e, P_total for SPARC/JET specifically) were not sourced -- these 4 reference points test "real geometry through generic transport," not each device's own actual predicted performance.
- Only NACA 4-digit airfoils tried (closed-form, simple) -- 5-digit and other real families (Eppler, Clark-Y, etc.) would need coordinate data from an external source rather than a formula, not attempted.
- The CST class function's forced closed trailing edge is a real, small, structural mismatch with NACA's actual open TE (confirmed ~0.0013c) -- affects every CST-fitted real airfoil in this project the same way, not particular to this reference set, and not worth fixing (would need a different class/shape function, a bigger change than this section's scope).

## 37. TORAX's generative models, built and run through the full eval battery -- three real infra gaps found on the way, and a genuine cross-domain divergence

Direct user request: "running all the models we want to run against torax... compare against those three tasks at least" -- cVAE/diffusion/GAN, evaluated the same way as VMEC++ (§18-20) and airfoils (§22-23): validity, axis-aligned steerability, arbitrary-direction steerability, all through `steerability_generic.py`. This section is largely about the THREE real integration gaps hit extending that script (already domain-agnostic in principle, per §30) to a genuinely new domain for the first time since it was built -- consistent with the project's own repeated finding that generalized abstractions mostly hold and the gaps are where the interesting work is (§31's own framing).

**Gap 1 -- a fidelity-convention inconsistency, introduced by §35's own bugfix.** Extending `steerability_generic.py`'s `SPEC_FACTORIES`/harness dispatch to `torax` surfaced that §35's fix to `torax_oracle.py`'s `_run_one` (resolving a fidelity NAME internally via `FIDELITY_PRESETS`) was itself inconsistent with the established convention every other domain follows: `vmec_oracle.py`/`airfoil_oracle.py`'s `params_to_worker_args`/`worker_fn` always receive an ALREADY-RESOLVED fidelity value (confirmed directly: `eval_cvae_steerability.py`, `eval_random_direction_steerability.py`, and `gym_schema.FidelityLevel(name, FIDELITY_PRESETS[name], ...)` all resolve the name before calling in). §35's fix worked for its one caller (`generate_torax_dataset.py`, which itself passed the raw string "low") but would have broken the next one -- `steerability_generic.py`'s gym_schema-based fidelity resolution already passes the resolved int (25), which §35's version of `_run_one` would have tried to look up as `FIDELITY_PRESETS[25]` and failed. **Reverted `_run_one`/`worker_fn`/`persistent_worker_fn` back to taking an already-resolved `n_rho` directly** (matching every other domain), and fixed the actual bug at the two call sites that were passing the raw string instead: `generate_torax_dataset.py` and `real_reference_torax.py` now both resolve `oracle.FIDELITY_PRESETS["low"]` themselves before calling `params_to_worker_args`. Confirmed identical real-reference output before/after (§36's 4-device numbers unchanged) -- purely a convention fix, not a behavior change. Added `persistent_worker_fn` as a new `DomainSpec` field (populated for `torax_spec()`) and a `torax` `Conditioning` entry (`extra_dim=0` -- no discrete/continuous aux at all in this domain) so `steerability_generic.py` could dispatch on `harness_fit` and use `oracle_harness_persistent.run_batch_persistent` instead of paying TORAX's JIT tax on every candidate.

**Gap 2 -- the `torax` and `train` Docker images each have half of what this eval needs.** `steerability_generic.py --domain torax` needs BOTH a trained PyTorch checkpoint (only in `train`) AND the real `torax` package (only in the standalone Python-3.12/numpy>=2 `torax` image, per §25's documented conflict with `train`'s numpy<2 pin) -- neither image alone can run it, discovered only by actually trying (`train` has no `torax` package; `torax` had no `torch`). Fixed by adding a CPU-only `torch` wheel to `Dockerfile.torax` -- safe because a modern torch build's numpy requirement is >=2-compatible (the numpy<2 pin lives on `Dockerfile.train`'s OLDER torch 2.2.0 build specifically, not a general torch/numpy incompatibility), confirmed directly (`numpy 2.5.2` + `torch 2.13.0+cpu` + `torax` all import cleanly together in the rebuilt image). Also needed `weights_only=False` on `steerability_generic.py`'s one `torch.load` call -- the newer torch version defaults that flag to `True` (a PyTorch 2.6+ default change), which rejected this project's own checkpoints (they store raw numpy arrays for `coeff_mean`/`coeff_std`, not just tensors) with an unpickling error; other scripts in this project (`gradient_walk.py`) already set this explicitly for the same reason.

**Gap 3 -- a real silent-corruption bug in the new TORAX training scripts, caught by checking computed stats rather than trusting that training ran without an error.** `coeffs_raw.std(axis=0)` on the float32 `X.npy` overflowed to `inf` for the `nbar` column specifically (values ~1e19-1e20; squared during variance computation, `1e20^2=1e40` exceeds float32's ~3.4e38 max) -- confirmed by directly inspecting the computed `coeff_std` array, not assumed safe just because `train_torax_cvae.py`'s first run completed and printed loss numbers. An `inf` std silently z-scores that entire column to exactly 0 for every row -- the model would have trained with zero signal on `nbar`, and `steerability_generic.py`'s un-normalization (`decoded * coeff_std + coeff_mean`) would have produced `inf`/`nan` overrides for every single generated candidate. **Fixed by computing `coeff_mean`/`coeff_std` in float64** (cast before `.mean()`/`.std()`) in all three `train_torax_*.py` scripts -- confirmed the overflow warning disappeared and retrained all three models. **A second, related, deeper finding surfaced while investigating the first**: `X.npy` contains rows with genuinely absurd geometry -- `R_major` up to 134m, `elongation_LCFS` up to 27, `B_0` up to 82T -- structurally-valid `ToraxConfig` overrides that still ran to a finite `SimError.NO_ERROR` (confirmed by direct inspection), nowhere near any real device (§36's real references all sit under `R_major`=6.2m, `B_0`=12.2T, elongation~1.7). This is the INPUT-side analogue of §35's flagged output-side extreme-value tail, not previously caught because nothing had looked at `X.npy`'s own marginal ranges before. Added a practical (generous, ~3-4x the largest real reference device, not a derived physical limit) per-parameter cap in `train_torax_cvae.py`'s `sanity_mask` (now `X`- and `Y`-aware, shared by all three training scripts) -- drops 1,532/15,275 rows (10.0%) combined, up from the target-only filter's 190 (1.2%). This lives in the training scripts rather than `gym_schema.py`'s `sanity_filter` because that field's established contract is `Y`-only (matches airfoil's own `cd`/`l_over_d` filter); extending it to see `X` too is flagged as a real future generalization, not done here to avoid a wider refactor.

**Models built**: `train_torax_cvae.py`/`train_torax_diffusion.py`/`train_torax_gan.py`, near-verbatim ports of the airfoil training scripts with `n_nfp=0` (no aux/conditioning beyond the target vector itself -- confirmed via `torax_oracle.py`'s own docstring and the new `Conditioning` entry) and the combined sanity filter above. 13,743 training rows after filtering. Params z-scored in LINEAR space, a flagged simplification -- TORAX's 10 params are themselves sampled multiplicatively/log-normally (§35), so a log-space z-scoring would likely fit their shape better, not done here to keep `steerability_generic.py`'s shared, domain-agnostic un-normalization step unchanged.

**Full eval battery, 12 anchors, step_std=1.0, seed=0, both direction modes, all three models:**

| model | validity | steer-conv (axis/arb) | correct-dir (axis/arb) | mean cosine (axis/arb) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| cVAE | 96.7% | 96.5% / 94.4% | 98.6% / 91.9% | 0.61 / 0.58 |
| Diffusion | 100.0% | 48.6% / 47.2% | 100.0% / 91.2% | 0.92 / 0.78 |
| GAN | 95.8% | 84.7% / 89.6% | 99.2% / 93.0% | 0.63 / 0.67 |

**Two real, honestly-reported findings, not yet mechanistically explained:**

1. **Every architecture's correct-direction rate here (91-100%) is far above any first-pass number seen in either earlier domain** (compare VMEC's own first single-seed pass, §18: cVAE 67.9%, diffusion 72.1%, GAN 72.9%, at the same step_std=1.0). Plausible but unconfirmed reasons: TORAX's 4-target space may simply have a cleaner/more monotonic parameter-to-target relationship than VMEC's 11 targets or airfoil's `cd`-heavy weak spot (§29/30's own "cd is the weak spot" pattern doesn't have an obvious TORAX analogue in this first pass); n_anchors=12 is a small sample, same caveat every first-pass number in this project has carried since §18-20's own noise-floor lesson (no rerun-across-seeds done yet for TORAX).

2. **Diffusion has perfect validity AND the strongest directional signal, but by far the worst steer-candidate convergence (~47-49% vs. cVAE's ~94-97% and GAN's ~85-90%).** This is a genuinely different profile from the GAN>diffusion>cVAE ranking that held consistently across every VMEC++/airfoil eval since §18 -- here diffusion's OWN unperturbed-target decodes are flawless (100% validity), but pushing its conditioning target away from the anchor produces a much higher rate of structurally-failing TORAX configs than either other architecture, even though the ones that DO converge are the most accurately steered of the three (cosine 0.92/0.78, clearly ahead of cVAE's 0.61/0.58 and GAN's 0.63/0.67). Read together with §34's own "coverage ranking is close to inverted from steerability ranking" finding for airfoils, this is a second instance of the same shape: this project's default single-number "which architecture is best" framing keeps turning out to depend heavily on which axis (validity vs. steered-convergence vs. directional-accuracy) is being asked about, not a stable global ranking -- flagged as a real pattern worth deliberately investigating across domains together, not chased further in this section.

**Open:**
- Single seed, single step_std, n_anchors=12 -- no noise-floor rerun (§2's own discipline) done for TORAX yet, so none of the numbers above should be trusted past the same ~±10pp band the rest of this project has established.
- No null-baseline (chance-level) control run for TORAX -- every number above is the TARGETED condition only; the diffusion convergence-rate finding in particular would benefit from knowing its null-mode convergence rate too.
- The input-side sanity filter (Gap 3) is a practical, not measured-physical, cap -- same caveat as §35's output-side one.
- No magnitude sweep (step_std) or coverage-metric (FILL/REACH, §33-34) run for TORAX yet -- both natural next steps now that real generative models exist for this domain.
- Params are still z-scored in linear space (flagged above) -- untested whether a log-space version changes any of these numbers.

## 38. Chasing §37's diffusion convergence gap to a real mechanism: catastrophic extrapolation into structurally-invalid geometry, not a numerical fluke

Direct user follow-up: "chase that a bit further" on §37's diffusion finding (perfect validity + strongest directional signal, but far worse steer-candidate convergence than cVAE/GAN). Three steps, each narrowing the question: a magnitude sweep, a null-mode control, then a mechanistic diagnostic that keeps the raw generated params and full failure reason instead of collapsing to ok/not-ok.

**Step 1 -- magnitude sweep (axis mode, same 12 anchors/seed, step_std in {0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0}), steer-candidate convergence:**

| model | 0.5 | 1.0 | 1.5 | 2.0 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| cVAE | 97.2% | 96.5% | 96.5% | 91.0% |
| Diffusion | 73.6% | 48.6% | 38.2% | 34.0% |
| GAN | 92.4% | 84.7% | 67.4% | 43.8% |

Answers the first question directly: diffusion's gap is NOT purely a "degrades faster at large magnitude" story -- it's already clearly worse than both other architectures at the SMALLEST step tried (73.6% vs. cVAE's 97.2%/GAN's 92.4% at step_std=0.5), and continues degrading faster too (roughly -25pp from 0.5->1.0 for diffusion vs. -0.7pp for cVAE and -7.6pp for GAN). Both things are true: diffusion is worse even close in, and it gets relatively worse faster further out. Diffusion's own steered-candidate cosine similarity stayed high throughout this sweep (0.797 at 0.5 -> 0.915 at 1.5 -> 0.919 at 2.0, from the same runs) -- the candidates that DO converge remain accurately steered at every magnitude tested, only the convergence rate itself craters.

**Step 2 -- null-mode control (step_std=1.0, but `steer_z = anchor_z` exactly, no push applied -- effectively re-decoding at the anchor's own real target).** Confirms the gap is specifically about moving the conditioning target, not an inherent diffusion sampling weakness: null-mode convergence is 98.6% for diffusion, closely matching its own validity (100.0%) and both other models' null numbers (cVAE 96.5%, GAN 94.4%) -- nothing like the ~48% seen once any real push (step_std=0.5+) is applied. The cliff appears immediately upon steering, not gradually from an already-weak baseline.

**Step 3 -- mechanistic diagnostic (`scripts/_diag_torax_diffusion_convergence.py`, new): kept the RAW decoded params and the oracle's full failure string for every candidate** (step_std=1.0, axis mode, same 12 anchors, k_steer=5 -> 240 candidates/model), cross-tabulated against `train_torax_cvae.py`'s own input-side sanity caps (§37 Gap 3):

| model | converged | failures violating a sanity cap | median R_major, converged/failed | median elongation, converged/failed | median Ip, converged/failed |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| cVAE | 97.1% (233/240) | 0/7 (0%) | 7.24 / 5.78 | 1.75 / 1.72 | 1.39e7 / 1.46e7 |
| Diffusion | 47.5% (114/240) | 100/126 (**79.4%**) | 5.04 / **170** | 1.49 / **-15** | 1.01e7 / **4.09e8** |
| GAN | 85.8% (206/240) | 7/34 (20.6%) | 5.99 / 6.14 | 1.83 / 2.07 | 1.19e7 / 3.51e6 |

**This is a real, clean mechanism, not a numerical fluke.** cVAE's rare failures (7/240) look geometrically indistinguishable from its successes -- it essentially never generates wild geometry even when pushed, consistent with its comparatively muted directional signal (cosine ~0.6, §37). GAN's failures (34/240) are mild misses -- similar median geometry to its successes, and the caps flag its SUCCESSES about as often as its failures (24.8% vs. 20.6%), meaning GAN occasionally produces mildly-out-of-cap-but-still-physically-fine designs rather than catastrophic ones. **Diffusion is categorically different: when it fails, it fails by generating physically nonsensical designs** -- a median `R_major` of 170m (vs. its own converged median of 5.04m and every real reference device in §36 sitting under 6.2m), a median elongation of **-15** (negative elongation isn't a real geometry at all -- this is why 122/126 of diffusion's failures are `structural (config build failed)`, rejected by TORAX's own Pydantic validation before the physics solver ever runs, not a solver-level `SimError`), and `Ip` up to a median of 409 MA among failures (~27x ITER's real 15MA). `R_major`/`a_minor`/`Ip`/`chi_e` are the most commonly violated params among diffusion's failures -- essentially the whole geometry+current block, not one isolated dimension.
