- Liquid coupling's real engineering constraint, sized directly rather than assumed: restarting `chtMultiRegionFoam` every few seconds of simulated time to step a Python-side lumped liquid ODE would cost many MINUTES per candidate (confirmed too slow for this project's dataset-generation scale, which currently runs ~130ms/candidate) -- a continuous in-solver coupling (a `codedFixedValue`/`codedMixed` boundary condition running the lumped ODE inline via OpenFOAM's own dynamic code compilation) is the right shape for this, not yet built.
- No radiative loss term ported yet (ambient convection is `H_AIR` alone, no linearized Stefan-Boltzmann addition).
- No decision made on the handle's own meshing strategy (`blockMesh` templating vs. `snappyHexMesh`) -- still open from §64.
- No dataset-generation-scale timing measured yet for this oracle (only single-candidate runs so far, each well under a second) -- real throughput at the ~130ms/candidate bar the self-written physics set is unconfirmed, not assumed.
- Still no decision/attempt to regenerate the dataset or retrain against this oracle -- correctly premature while 2 of 5 targets are entirely missing.

## 66. Liquid energy-balance coupling -- `temp_at_2h_C` for real, a real blocker found and routed around, and a real bug caught by comparing against v6

Direct continuation on "let's keep going." Addresses §65's first two "Open" bullets: `temp_at_2h_C` (this domain's own headline target) was entirely missing, and the sizing already done there (a Python-side per-timestep restart loop costs many MINUTES per candidate, confirmed too slow) pointed at "a continuous in-solver coupling... running the lumped ODE inline via OpenFOAM's own dynamic code compilation" as the right shape. That specific mechanism (`codedMixed`) turned out to be a dead end -- the real fix ended up being better than the original plan, not just a workaround.

**The blocker, found by trying it, not by reading docs first:** wrote a `codedMixed` boundary condition (Robin/convective law with a time-varying `Ta` this project's own inline C++ integrates from the liquid's energy balance each timestep) into a small standalone test case. `chtMultiRegionFoam` refused outright: `dynamicCode::checkSecurity()` calls `isAdministrator()` unconditionally, before even checking its own `allowSystemOperations` switch -- confirmed by reading `$FOAM_SRC/OpenFOAM/db/dynamicLibrary/dynamicCode/dynamicCode.C` directly, not assumed from the error text. There is no override. This project's `openfoam` service runs as root (§65's own deliberate choice, matching `train`/`oracle`'s convention) -- so `codedMixed`/`codedFixedValue` are unusable here, full stop, regardless of how the code inside them is written.

**The fix: a real compiled OpenFOAM plugin instead of runtime-compiled inline code.** `scripts/openfoam_libs/liquidCoupling/` is a small `wmake libso` library (`liquidCouplingFvPatchScalarField.H`/`.C`, `Make/files`, `Make/options`) implementing a `mixed`-derived boundary condition -- structurally identical to OpenFOAM's own built-in `externalWallHeatFluxTemperature` (its `updateCoeffs()` was read directly out of `$FOAM_SRC` before writing this, giving the exact Robin-BC algebra: `refValue=Ta`, `valueFraction=h/(h+kappa*deltaCoeffs)`), except `Ta` is a per-run lumped liquid temperature (`Tliquid_`, real member data, not a fragile function-local static) integrated forward one explicit-Euler step per real solver timestep from `m*c*dT/dt = -sum(h*(Tliquid-Tface)*faceArea)`. This is a **better** fix than the original plan, not merely a workaround: it compiles once at image-build time (`Dockerfile.openfoam`'s new `RUN wmake libso .` step, output straight into `$FOAM_LIBBIN` so it's on every case's library search path automatically) instead of once per candidate, which matters directly for dataset-generation throughput -- a per-candidate `g++` invocation would have cost real wall-clock time on every single row.

**Validated against an INDEPENDENT reference before touching the real oracle, same discipline as §64/§65:** `reference_1d_coupled.py` extends `reference_1d.py`'s own from-scratch 1D solver with the same liquid ODE. This is a genuine cross-check, not a restatement of the same formula: the flux law used (`G_left*(Tliquid_prev - T0_prev)`, i.e. film resistance in series with the half-cell conduction resistance) is provably the exact same quantity a converged OpenFOAM `mixed` BC's own voltage-divider algebra computes at its boundary face -- two independently written pieces of code (a from-scratch tridiagonal FD solve vs. OpenFOAM's FVM/PIMPLE machinery) implementing the identical physical law. Built a standalone `coupled_spike/` case (same slab/material as `slab_case/`, `liquidCoupling` on the `liquid` patch) to check it:

| quantity | reference (`reference_1d_coupled.py`) | OpenFOAM (`liquidCoupling`) |
|---|--:|--:|
| liquid T at 60s | 89.977 C | 89.976 C |
| liquid T at 2h | 89.499 C | 89.498 C |
| outer-surface T at 60s | 61.72 C | 61.65 C |
| outer-surface T at 2h | 75.49 C | 75.40 C |

Liquid temperature matches to <0.001C; the outer-surface residual (~0.07-0.1C) is the same timestep-discretization artifact §64 already characterized, not a new issue.

**A real bug, caught only by comparing against v6, not by the isolated validation above:** wired `liquidCoupling` into `mug_openfoam_oracle.py`'s actual wedge-mesh case and ran the same 4 test cases from §65. First result: `temp_at_2h_C` came back ~88-89C for every case -- i.e. the liquid had barely cooled at all in 2 hours, wildly implausible. Root cause: the wedge mesh's `liquid` patch is a 2.5-degree-half-angle SLICE of the real mug, so `gSum(q*magSf())` inside `liquidCoupling` was only summing that slice's own heat loss -- about 1/72nd of the real total -- while `mLiquid` was (correctly) the full mug's physical liquid mass. Heat loss under-counted by ~72x, liquid barely cools. The standalone `coupled_spike/` validation above never caught this because that case is a flat slab with a full-area patch, not a wedge -- a genuinely different code path, worth remembering when trusting a validation case that doesn't share the real oracle's own geometry. Fixed with an explicit `wedgeFraction` dictionary entry (`360/(2*half_angle_deg)`, defaults to 1 so `coupled_spike/` is unaffected) the BC multiplies its patch-local sum by before integrating the ODE.

**Re-validated against v6 across the same 4 cases, discrepancy explained, not hand-waved:**

| case | `temp_at_2h_C` (openfoam / v6) | `touch_temp_60s_C` (openfoam / v6) |
|---|--:|--:|
| polypropylene | 46.55 / 29.60 | 58.98 / 57.00 |
| polystyrene | 28.66 / 22.39 | 67.47 / 63.81 |
| copper | 49.17 / 31.63 | 44.90 / 45.47 |
| stainless steel 304 | 46.44 / 30.78 | 54.48 / 55.15 |

`temp_at_2h_C` now lands in the physically right REGIME (liquid cooling substantially toward ambient over 2h, not staying pinned near 90C) but runs consistently higher than v6 by 6-18C. Both of this oracle's own already-documented gaps point in exactly this direction, not new mysteries: no radiative loss term (v6's linearized Stefan-Boltzmann addition would pull MORE heat out through the outer wall, cooling the liquid faster) and no handle (a real extra surface losing heat to ambient, and extra thermal mass, that v6 has and this oracle doesn't). The pattern across materials is consistent with this explanation, not just directionally lucky: polystyrene (poor conductor, cool outer surface, radiation negligible either way) has the SMALLEST gap (6.3C); copper (best conductor, hottest outer surface, where a T^4 radiative term matters most) has the LARGEST (17.6C).

**Timing**: ~0.7-0.8s per candidate (up slightly from §65's <1s, from the extra 2-hour transient now needing genuine liquid-side bookkeeping every step) -- still a single-candidate number, not a measured batch throughput, but a much more useful data point than before: at this rate a 20K-row dataset is ~4 hours single-threaded, plausibly 15-30 minutes with reasonable parallelism, a very different picture than the abandoned restart-loop approach's per-candidate MINUTES estimate.

**A user double-check, confirmed by direct computation, not just re-reading code**: does wall thickness actually feed into anything? Walking `t_wall` from 0.5mm to 10mm on a fixed inner-cavity shape: `mass_kg` scales correctly (0.0095 to 0.2161, ~23x) and the OpenFOAM mesh has real radial cells spanning `r_inner` to `r_outer` at each height (verified concretely, e.g. 30.00mm->33.00mm at the base of a real 3/5mm-wall case) -- not a degenerate zero-thickness shell. But `liquid_volume_L` stayed EXACTLY 0.3426L across the whole 0.5-10mm sweep. This is not a bug: `r_base_mm/r_mid_mm/r_rim_mm` are defined as the INNER cavity radii in this whole domain (v6 included, confirmed identical well before this OpenFOAM work -- it's exactly why `liquid_volume_L` matched exactly in every §65/§66 validation table), and wall thickness grows the mug OUTWARD from there, never eating into the cavity. Flagged explicitly to the user (a real, if perhaps non-obvious, modeling choice -- two mugs with identical `r_base/mid/rim` but very different thickness hold identically the same liquid); user's call was to leave the convention as-is.

**Open**:
- No radiative loss term yet (identified above as a real, quantified contributor to the `temp_at_2h_C` gap, not just a documented gap in the abstract) -- likely the next cheapest win, since it doesn't need new meshing, just a term in the `ambient` patch's BC.
- No handle yet -- still the bigger, meshing-strategy-dependent gap (`blockMesh` templating vs. `snappyHexMesh`, open since §64).
- `handle_temp_60s_C` still entirely undefined without a handle.
- Still only a single-candidate timing number, not a real measured batch throughput at dataset-generation scale.
- Still no dataset regen/retrain -- still correctly premature while `handle_temp_60s_C` is missing and the wall-only oracle's `temp_at_2h_C`/`touch_temp_60s_C` gap against v6 hasn't been closed or explicitly accepted.

## 67. Radiative loss -- OpenFOAM already had it built in, no custom code needed at all

Direct continuation ("yes please - radiative loss"). Expected this to need another small coded/compiled BC extension, same shape as §66. It didn't: `externalWallHeatFluxTemperature` (already in use on the `ambient` patch since §65) has its own `emissivity` dictionary entry. Read its `updateCoeffs()` again (same file §66 already pulled from `$FOAM_SRC`) and algebraically expanded the emissivity branch's `h + eps*sigma*((Ta^3+Tp^3) + Ta*Tp*(Ta+Tp))` term -- it factors to exactly `h + eps*sigma*(Ta+Tp)*(Ta^2+Tp^2)`, which is IDENTICAL to `thermal_mug_spike.py`'s own `_radiative_h` linearized-Stefan-Boltzmann coefficient, right down to the same one-timestep-lagged linearization convention (both use the patch/wall's own previous-iterate temperature, not the current unknown). Added one line -- `emissivity {tms.EMISSIVITY_SURFACE};` (0.9, same constant v6 uses) -- to the `ambient` patch's dict in `mug_openfoam_oracle.py`. No plugin changes, no recompilation, no new validation case needed (the underlying BC and its radiative math are OpenFOAM's own, not this project's).

**Re-validated against v6, same 4 cases as §65/§66:**

| case | `temp_at_2h_C` (no-radiation §66 / with-radiation §67 / v6) | `touch_temp_60s_C` (§66 / §67 / v6) |
|---|---|---|
| polypropylene | 46.55 / 38.16 / 29.60 | 58.98 / 56.92 / 57.00 |
| polystyrene | 28.66 / 23.69 / 22.39 | 67.47 / 63.67 / 63.81 |
| copper | 49.17 / 40.86 / 31.63 | 44.90 / 44.77 / 45.47 |
| stainless steel 304 | 46.44 / 37.29 / 30.78 | 54.48 / 54.17 / 55.15 |

The radiative term closed roughly half the remaining `temp_at_2h_C` gap in every case (e.g. polypropylene 16.95C->8.56C, copper 17.54C->9.23C), and `touch_temp_60s_C` moved measurably closer to v6 too even though that number is dominated by conduction, not radiation -- both consistent with adding a real missing heat-loss pathway rather than an unrelated parameter tweak. Polystyrene's `temp_at_2h_C` is now within 1.3C of v6 (down from 6.3C) -- exactly the case §66 predicted would improve LEAST from radiation (poor conductor, coolest outer surface, radiation matters least there) yet still improved the most in absolute closeness to v6, because it was already the smallest-gap case to begin with. The remaining gap still tracks conductivity the same way §66 found (largest for copper/steel) -- consistent with the handle (still missing) being the dominant remaining explanation, not a new mystery.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14).

**Open** (unchanged from §66 otherwise): no handle yet (still the bigger, meshing-strategy-dependent gap), `handle_temp_60s_C` still undefined, still only single-candidate timing, still no dataset regen/retrain -- correctly premature while `handle_temp_60s_C` is missing.

## 68. The handle -- `handle_temp_60s_C` for real, no snappyHexMesh needed, and it turned out v6's own model didn't need real 3D CAD fidelity to replicate

Direct continuation ("handle next for sure"). §64 left the handle's meshing strategy as an open decision between `blockMesh` templating and `snappyHexMesh`. Neither was actually needed: v6's own handle model, re-read carefully before building anything, is already a strong simplification -- a 1D fin (cross-section area `A_h`, perimeter `P_h`) whose BOTH ends attach to the SAME single wall node (`patch_local`, the wall's fixed theta=0/mid-height outer temperature) via a contact conductance `H_HANDLE_CONTACT=200 W/(m2 K)`, losing heat laterally to ambient along its length via `H_AIR*P_h`. That fidelity level doesn't need a real 3D CAD attachment at all.

**Design**: the handle is its own SEPARATE solid region/case, not a snappyHexMesh attachment onto the wedge. A straight SQUARE-cross-section rod (`side=sqrt(A_h)`, chosen to preserve the real round handle's cross-sectional area exactly -- same axial conduction resistance and thermal mass -- at the honestly quantified cost of ~12.8% more perimeter/lateral-loss area than the real round shape: `4*sqrt(A_h) / (pi*d_h) = 2/sqrt(pi) ~ 1.128`, not silently absorbed), meshed with a single plain `blockMesh` hex block (`(N_HANDLE_AXIAL, 1, 1)`, no wedge -- nothing about a straight rod is axisymmetric). `_write_common_solid_dicts()` factors out the region/thermophysical/radiation/fvSchemes/fvSolution boilerplate the wall and handle cases share, so the two can't silently drift apart.

**Coupling: one real OpenFOAM run, then a second, sequential, one-way-coupled one -- no multi-region conjugate coupling built.** The wall case now also carries a `patchProbes` functionObject (standard OpenFOAM sampling, not custom code) reading its own `ambient` patch's temperature at the wedge's mid-height, at every solver step, into `postProcessing/wallMidProbe/solid/0/T`. After the wall run finishes, that time series becomes the handle case's own `Ta` -- as a `table` Function1 (again a standard, built-in OpenFOAM mechanism, not custom code) on BOTH the handle's `root` and `tip` end patches, replicating v6's own single-attachment-point simplification exactly. This is a one-way coupling (the handle doesn't feed back into the wall's own solve) -- and not an arbitrary corner cut: the wall's wedge mesh has only ONE cell azimuthally (§65's own no-azimuthal-variation scope), so there is no specific "wedge column" for a handle's small heat sink to attach back onto even if two-way coupling were wanted here. `handle_temp_60s_C` itself comes from a second, ordinary `probes` functionObject at the handle's own mid-length point, matching v6's `handle_mid_idx = N_HANDLE//2` convention.

**Validated against v6, same 4 cases, default handle (60mm/8mm natural_rubber)**:

| case | `handle_temp_60s_C` (openfoam / v6) | mass_kg (openfoam / v6) |
|---|---|---|
| polypropylene | 20.000 / 20.000000001 | 0.0823 / 0.0779 |
| polystyrene | 20.000 / 20.000000004 | 0.0239 / 0.0226 |
| copper | 20.000 / 20.000000005 | 3.2290 / 3.0782 |
| stainless steel 304 | 20.000 / 20.000000007 | 1.0709 / 1.0081 |

`handle_temp_60s_C` matches to 6+ decimal places in every case -- not a coincidence, and not a stuck/broken value either (see below): with `H_HANDLE_CONTACT=200` and this handle's length/material, essentially no heat has propagated past the contact resistance into the rod within 60s, in BOTH implementations independently. Checked this isn't a trivial always-ambient result by swapping in a short (20mm), fat (15mm), high-conductivity (aluminum) handle on the same wall case: `handle_temp_60s_C` came back 24.84C, a real, physically sensible response to geometry and material, not a stuck constant. `mass_kg`'s incremental handle contribution (`0.0823-0.0795=0.0028kg` for polypropylene) matches `A_h*L_h*rho_handle` computed by hand (0.002775kg) almost exactly -- the remaining wall-only discrepancy is exactly the same pre-existing inner-vs-outer-surface-area approximation §65 already reconciled, unaffected by adding the handle.

**Timing**: ~1.6-1.75s per candidate (up from §67's ~0.7-0.8s -- the handle case is a second, separate `chtMultiRegionFoam` invocation with its own blockMesh/solve/probe-parsing overhead, roughly doubling per-candidate cost). Still a single-candidate number, not a measured batch throughput.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14).

**Open**:
- No two-way handle<->wall thermal coupling (documented above as a fidelity match to the wall mesh's own azimuthal resolution, not a shortcut -- would need a real multi-region conjugate interface and a wedge mesh that actually resolves an azimuthal handle location to be worth building).
- No radiative loss on the handle itself (matches v6, which doesn't model this either).
- Still only single-candidate timing (~1.6-1.75s now, two solver invocations per candidate) -- no real measured batch throughput yet.
- All 5 targets (`temp_at_2h_C`, `mass_kg`, `touch_temp_60s_C`, `handle_temp_60s_C`, `liquid_volume_L`) now exist for the first time. Still no dataset regen/retrain against this oracle -- the wall's own `temp_at_2h_C`/`touch_temp_60s_C` gap against v6 (radiation-explained remainder, largest for high-conductivity materials, §66/§67) hasn't been closed or explicitly accepted as good enough to commit to.

## 69. A real course-correction on validation philosophy, a full 360-degree mesh, and a 2-pass handle<->wall coupling for actual visualization use

**User feedback, direct and important**: "I just want to make sure we don't overindex on matching v6... It's not like I was incredibly happy with that and how things were working... Just really try to make it a good quality visualization." This is a real correction to how §65-68 were framed -- v6 is the self-written solver the user explicitly ordered replaced (§64), never a trusted ground truth, just the prior implementation. Treating "closer to v6" as a success signal in those sections was the wrong framing (even though the underlying validation work -- independent references, real OpenFOAM physics -- was sound). Saved as a standing memory so future sessions on this domain don't repeat it. Going forward: v6 agreement is at most a sanity check, not a target.

**Concretely, the ask was to visualize the real OpenFOAM temperature field** in the existing mug viewer (`viewer/src/pages/mug.astro`, which already has a genuinely good Three.js `LatheGeometry` body mesh with per-vertex colors driven by a real temperature grid, animated over a time slider, plus handle/liquid/rim meshes -- currently fed by a live `simulate_v6()` call through `mug_physics_service.py`, ~1.5s/request). Decided: replace v6 as the live backend entirely (not a toggle), and -- since the wedge mesh (§65-68) has no real azimuthal resolution, so no honest local handle-cooling effect to show -- extend the mesh to a real full 360-degree revolve rather than fake the detail.

**`_write_full_block_mesh_dict` (new)**: N_THETA=24 angular sectors (matching v6's own resolution for comparable visual granularity, NOT to chase v6 parity -- explicitly not the goal anymore), each an exact repeat of the old single wedge block's structure, vertices shared between neighbors so blockMesh treats the seams as ordinary internal faces -- no `wedge` patches, no `mergePatchPairs`, no snappyHexMesh. `checkMesh` confirms a clean, closed, valid mesh (4608 cells, "Mesh OK", non-orthogonality/skewness all in the OK range). Sector i=0 is centered on theta=0 (matching v6's/mug.astro's own fixed +X handle placement convention) and its outer-surface faces are pulled into their own `ambientHandle` patch, separate from the rest of the ring's `ambient` -- the concrete hook the handle's local effect acts on.

**Real two-way coupling was investigated and explicitly NOT built this pass**: the architecturally "correct" OpenFOAM-native mechanism is `mappedWall` + `turbulentTemperatureCoupledBaffleMixed` (real inter-region conjugate coupling) -- but it wants the two regions' meshes to actually touch at a matching interface, and neither of the two ways to get there (mesh the wall+handle as one conformal mesh, or hand-build mapped-patch metadata for independently-meshed regions) could be validated without open-ended trial and error. Given direct user steer toward visualization value over physics purity, chose instead:

**A 2-pass approximation, real OpenFOAM physics on both passes, no new custom numerics**:
1. Pass 1: full wall solve, `ambientHandle` identical to `ambient` -- exists only to feed `wallMidProbe` (a `patchProbes` functionObject, standard OpenFOAM, on the `ambientHandle` patch specifically now) into the handle case, same mechanism as §68.
2. Pass 2: the handle case (unchanged structure from §68), now ALSO carrying a `rootProbe` (`patchProbes` on its own `root` patch) -- gives the handle's own root-face temperature over time. Zipped index-by-index against pass 1's wall temperature (both series step through the identical deltaT sequence from identical controlDicts, so index-pairing is robust; float-matching on time values isn't needed and wasn't used) to compute `H_HANDLE_CONTACT*(T_wall-T_root)` at every sample -- the handle's REAL heat draw -- then time-averaged into one representative `q_handle_draw` (~564 W/m^2 for the default rubber handle -- a real, not tiny, number).
3. Pass 3: wall solve again, this time with `ambientHandle` given an EXTRA `q` entry (`externalWallHeatFluxTemperature`'s own built-in flux parameter -- not custom code) equal to `-q_handle_draw` (negative = extra loss, matching the BC's own sign convention, confirmed against $FOAM_SRC same as §66/§67). This pass's results are authoritative.

Constant `-500 W/m^2` test confirmed the mechanism produces a real, visible local cool patch (`ambientHandle` min/max 31.6-51.7C vs the rest of the ring's 37.3-57.2C at t=60s) before trusting the real computed value; the actual `q_handle_draw` (~564 W/m^2) is comparably sized, so the real run should show an even more visible effect. `temp_at_2h_C` (a global, not just local, quantity) moved from 38.17C (pass 1, no local sink) to 37.13C (pass 3, with it) -- a small but real, correctly-signed whole-liquid response to a small local extra loss path, not a rounding artifact.

**Housekeeping**: the old single-wedge `_write_block_mesh_dict`/`WEDGE_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` are removed (fully superseded, not left as dead code); `liquidCoupling`'s `wedgeFraction` is no longer needed (the `liquid` patch is the real full circle now, so `gSum(q*magSf())` already sums the true total -- defaults to 1 as if unset).

**Timing**: ~6.8s/candidate (up from §68's ~1.6-1.75s -- two full N_THETA=24 wall solves instead of one N_THETA=1 wedge solve, plus the handle solve). A real cost increase from the mesh-refinement + extra pass, not yet weighed against dataset-generation-scale needs -- explicitly a tradeoff made for visualization quality per this section's own framing, not something to silently justify against a throughput target that hasn't been discussed since.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14).

**Open**:
- The viewer itself (`mug.astro`/`mug_physics_service.py`) is NOT wired to this new oracle yet -- this section only builds the physics side. Next: export the full `T_wall_outer`-shaped field (all theta, multiple time snapshots) from the wall's own boundary field files and swap the live backend.
- `q_handle_draw` is a single time-averaged constant, not a real time-varying flux -- an explicit, documented simplification of the 2-pass design (see above), not a claim of full time-accuracy.
- No two-way conjugate coupling (still using the 2-pass approximation, by direct choice, not as a stopgap awaiting a "real" fix).
- ~6.8s/candidate has not been checked against any throughput target -- deliberately not a concern for this section's own goal (visualization), but will matter again if/when dataset regeneration comes back into scope.

## 70. Mug domain v2, Phase A step 1 -- proof that a real geometric feasibility gate is possible at all, via a genuine blockMesh failure, not a hand-rolled checker

Direct user feedback, and a significant one: the current parameterization (`r_base/r_mid/r_rim` inner radii, thickness always added outward) makes EVERY positive parameter combination a valid, watertight design -- there's no way to sample something that should be rejected. User: *"the parameterization shouldn't guarantee it... we should have some test of like... did this actually make a watertight mug that can hold water."* Also flagged the handle as a "weird donut floating in space" with no real geometric or thermal joint. `gym_schema.py`'s own `mug_spec()` docstring already named this exact gap: *"mug_oracle.py has NO non-convergence failure mode at all... `ok` is only ever False on a structurally invalid input, not a solver failure"* -- unlike VMEC++/XFOIL, where infeasibility is the solver's own convergence failure, not a hand-coded check.

Wrote a full plan (`/home/sl8rv/.claude/plans/magical-bouncing-corbato.md`, via EnterPlanMode/ExitPlanMode given how much this touches) for a staged 3-phase redesign: **Phase A** -- a genuinely free-form body (`r_inner(z)`/`r_outer(z)` as independent splines, no longer coupled by an `outer=inner+thickness` construction, so they CAN cross) with feasibility coming from whether the mesh actually generates, not a bespoke checker. **Phase B** -- the handle becomes its own rich spline-arc shape with real attach points that must land on the body's surface (a real "floating handle" rejection). **Phase C** -- the joint becomes a first-class categorical material slot with a geometry-derived contact conductance, replacing the fixed `H_HANDLE_CONTACT=200` constant. Confirmed along the way that NONE of the generic scaffolding needs to change for any of this: `gym_schema.py`'s `validity_fn` and `generate_mug_dataset.py`'s existing accept/reject batch loop (hit-rate logging already built in) already fully support a domain with real rejection -- VMEC++'s own spec already uses `validity_fn=None` and folds infeasibility straight into `ok`, exactly the pattern `mug_openfoam_oracle.py` already uses (`dict(valid=False)` on any OpenFOAM failure). The work is entirely in the geometry and the oracle, not the harness.

**This section is Phase A's first concrete step**: confirmed OpenFOAM's real `spline` blockMeshDict edge type (Catmull-Rom, `$FOAM_SRC/mesh/blockMesh/blockEdges/splineEdge/splineEdge.H`, `TypeName("spline")`) exists and works, and syntax pulled straight from the source constructor (`spline v0 v1 ( (x y z) ... );`, endpoints NOT repeated in the point list -- confirmed via `appendEndPoints()`, not guessed) since no shipped OF10 tutorial actually uses spline edges to copy from. Built a standalone spike (`scripts/openfoam_mug_spike/spline_body_spike/build_case.py`, not wired into the real oracle yet) -- a thin wedge (cheapest possible mesh to isolate the spline-edge mechanic alone, not the full §69 revolve) whose 4 axial edges are spline curves through a real 6-point `r_inner(z)`/`r_outer(z)` profile, genuinely wavy (a bulge and a pinch), not the old 2-breakpoint piecewise-linear shape.

**Result -- the actual proof this phase exists to get**:
- A feasible profile (`r_outer(z) > r_inner(z)` everywhere) meshes cleanly: `blockMesh` succeeds, `checkMesh` reports "Mesh OK".
- The SAME code path, given a profile where `r_outer` dips BELOW `r_inner` around the mid-height (a shape the OLD parameterization could never even express, let alone generate), makes `blockMesh` FAIL outright: `"Wedge patch 'wedgeNeg' is not planar... the normal... differs from the average normal... by 0.444444"`. A real geometric failure from the real meshing tool, caught by the SAME `except foam.OpenFOAMError: return dict(valid=False)` pattern already used throughout `mug_openfoam_oracle.py` since §65 -- no new validity-checking code needed, just geometry expressive enough to actually reach that existing path.

Honest caveat: this spike's specific failure mode (wedge-patch non-planarity) is an artifact of using a WEDGE mesh for the spike's own cheapness -- the real oracle uses §69's full 360-degree revolve (no wedge patches at all), so the real failure mode there will likely be a different blockMesh diagnostic (e.g. negative cell volume/concave block) for an equivalent crossing profile. The QUALITATIVE result (a genuinely bad profile makes blockMesh fail, full stop) is what this step set out to prove, not the exact error text.

**Open** (Phase A, not yet done): the new body-only parameter module (`PARAM_NAMES`/`PARAM_DIM` for spline control points + material, reusing `thermal_mug_spike.py`'s material tables/constants directly, not reimplementing them), wiring spline edges into the REAL full-revolve oracle mesh (`_write_full_block_mesh_dict`'s 4 axial edges per angular sector, not just one isolated wedge), a fast Python-side pre-check (sample the splines densely and reject crossings before ever invoking OpenFOAM, for cheap rejection at dataset-generation scale), and confirming `generate_mug_dataset.py`'s existing accept/reject loop shows a real, non-trivial hit rate once wired up -- none of this attempted yet, checking in with the user before continuing further into Phase A given how much of a redesign this already is.

## 71. Mug domain v2, real pivot: dropping the revolve entirely for a full 3D free-form body, meshed with snappyHexMesh -- superseding §70's spline-revolve approach

Direct, important user pushback on §70's own direction, immediately after it: *"we really don't want this to be revolve-based. That's a core issue actually... something *can be* radially symmetric, but there's a real lack of anything interesting happening when we enforce that."* This isn't a refinement of §70 -- a body of revolution (§70's `blockMesh` full-revolve, no matter how the profile curve is parameterized) can never express real asymmetry. Re-entered plan mode (the redesign's blast radius and now a real tool-choice change both warranted a fresh sign-off) and updated the plan (`/home/sl8rv/.claude/plans/magical-bouncing-corbato.md`) with the user: the body becomes a full 3D free-form-deformation (FFD) shell, and `snappyHexMesh` (OpenFOAM's real tool for meshing arbitrary geometry from a surface, `blockMesh`'s structured multi-block approach being fundamentally unable to hand-author an asymmetric topology) replaces `blockMesh` for the body's volume mesh -- confirmed via the real `tutorials/mesh/snappyHexMesh/flange` tutorial before writing anything, same discipline as always.

**`scripts/openfoam_mug_spike/ffd_body_spike/ffd_geometry.py` (new, pure geometry, no solver)**: independent inner/outer FFD lattices (a coarse 3D grid of control-point displacements, e.g. 2x2x3) deform two base cylinders via piecewise MULTILINEAR interpolation (a deliberate simplicity choice over classic Bezier/Bernstein-blended FFD -- no overshoot between control points, easy to reason about, a real if simpler lattice-deformer technique, e.g. Blender's own lattice modifier supports this same linear mode). Zero displacement recovers a plain cylinder (a sanity baseline); nonzero displacement produces genuinely ASYMMETRIC shapes -- confirmed directly: one test deformation made the outer radius at a single height range from 31mm to 40mm depending on angle, something no revolve-based representation could ever produce. Tessellates both deformed surfaces into a single watertight multi-solid STL (`outerSurface`/`innerSurface`/`bottom`/`top`), reusing the EXACT SAME computed ring arrays across adjacent triangulated patches so shared edges are bit-identical, not independently re-derived -- genuine watertightness, not an approximation of it.

**Feasibility gate #1, calibrated against real behavior, not assumed**: OpenFOAM's `surfaceCheck -checkSelfIntersection` on the generated STL. Found and had to work through a real false-positive risk before trusting it: the perfectly-flat, exactly-symmetric zero-displacement baseline itself reports 4 self-intersections (a numerical degeneracy specific to that one perfectly-flat special case, not a general property of valid designs) -- while a genuinely deformed but VALID asymmetric shape (the 31-40mm bulge above) reports ZERO, and a deliberately-crossing shape (outer surface pulled inward past the inner one) reports 171. A real, usable, well-separated signal (0 vs 171), calibrated by testing three real cases rather than trusting the tool's raw output at face value.

**Feasibility gate #2, and a genuinely important caveat found by testing the bad case, not assumed to work**: ran the full real pipeline (`blockMesh` background box -> `surfaceFeatures` -> `snappyHexMesh -overwrite` -> `checkMesh`, syntax pulled from the `flange` tutorial) on both the valid bulge case and the deliberately-crossing case. The valid case succeeded cleanly on the first real attempt: "Mesh OK", real named patches (`body_outerSurface`/`body_innerSurface`/`body_bottom`/`body_top`) present, the background box's own `allBoundary` patch fully REMOVED (meaning the "inside" point correctly identified a properly enclosed solid region). The crossing case did NOT hard-fail the way §70's `blockMesh` wedge case did -- `snappyHexMesh` completed "without any errors" and `checkMesh` only reported one soft quality warning (skewness), which alone would have been an unreliable, easy-to-miss feasibility signal. The REAL tell: the crossing case's `allBoundary` patch was NOT removed (survived with 3612 real faces) -- meaning the flood-fill from the inside point leaked out into the background domain, a direct sign the body surface did not properly enclose a bounded solid region. Confirmed this discriminates cleanly by re-running the valid case: `allBoundary` removed, "Mesh OK" true, no `allBoundary` patch in the final `checkMesh` patch list -- all three false for the valid case, all three (survives / soft-fail / patch present) true for the bad one. The real feasibility gate for this new mechanism is therefore: `-checkSelfIntersection` count above a small threshold (cheap, catches most bad cases before ever running snappyHexMesh) OR the background `allBoundary` patch surviving snappyHexMesh (catches whatever slips past the first check) -- not just "does snappyHexMesh exit nonzero," which this test proved is NOT sufficient on its own.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14 -- this section touches nothing the existing suite covers).

**Open**: wiring these two feasibility checks into an actual oracle function (`valid=False` on either), the thermal BCs/materials on the snappyHexMesh output (liquidCoupling on `innerSurface`, radiative ambient on `outerSurface`), a real `PARAM_NAMES`/`PARAM_DIM` module for the lattice-displacement parameter vector, and confirming `generate_mug_dataset.py`-style batch sampling shows a real non-trivial hit rate -- all per the plan's Phase A1/A2, none attempted yet. Explicit checkpoint with the user before continuing, per the plan's own stated intent, given how much ground this section alone covered.

## 72. Mug domain v2, Phase A1 -- a real, working end-to-end oracle for the FFD body: geometry, both feasibility gates, and real thermal physics, all wired together

Direct continuation ("keep going"). Promoted §71's spike geometry (`ffd_geometry.py`) to a first-class module, `scripts/mug_ffd_geometry.py` (the spike's own copy deleted, not duplicated -- the spike scripts now import the real one, so there's exactly one copy to keep correct). Added `wall_and_cavity_volumes()` for `mass_kg`/`liquid_volume_L` -- NOT via the divergence-theorem sum over the (open, uncapped) lateral surface alone, which was tried first and is only valid for a properly closed manifold; switched to cross-sectional polygon area (shoelace formula, works for any FFD-deformed non-circular cross-section, not just circles) integrated along z by the trapezoidal rule. Checked directly against the known analytic cylinder volume (`pi*r^2*h`) for the zero-displacement case before trusting it on deformed shapes: agreed to within ~0.3% (pure discretization error from the theta/z sampling resolution, not a formula bug).

**`scripts/mug_openfoam_oracle_v2.py` (new)** -- a complete, working `simulate()`: build the FFD-deformed STL -> §71's two feasibility gates (self-intersection count threshold, then the background `allBoundary` patch surviving snappyHexMesh) -> copy `constant/polyMesh` to `constant/solid/polyMesh` (a plain directory copy, needed because neither `snappyHexMesh` nor `surfaceFeatures` support a `-region` flag the way `blockMesh` does -- confirmed via `-help`, not assumed -- so region-based meshing for `chtMultiRegionFoam` has to be assembled this way rather than generated natively per-region) -> the SAME `liquidCoupling` (§66) and radiative `externalWallHeatFluxTemperature` (§67) physics as the old revolve oracle, now attached to snappyHexMesh's own automatically-named patches (`body_innerSurface`/`body_outerSurface`/`body_bottom`/`body_top`, the geometry entry's own name prefixed onto each STL `solid` block) instead of hand-authored block boundary faces. `PARAM_NAMES`/`worker_fn`/`params_to_worker_args` match this project's usual oracle convention (`mug_oracle.py`'s own shape) so wiring into `oracle_harness`/`generate_mug_dataset.py` later (Phase A2) is a drop-in, not a redesign -- not done yet, per the plan's own staging.

**Result -- the real, complete pipeline validated end to end**:
- A real design (asymmetric bulge, polypropylene, 3mm nominal wall): `valid=True`, `touch_temp_60s_C=80.7C`, `temp_at_2h_C=36.0C`, `mass_kg=0.0647`, `liquid_volume_L=0.28L` -- 28.6s (one candidate, unoptimized; not yet measured at batch scale).
- The SAME deliberately-crossing geometry from §71, run through the REAL `simulate()` this time (not the standalone spike): rejected in 0.07s, `reason="self-intersecting surface (171 locations)"` -- the cheap gate catching it before any OpenFOAM invocation at all, exactly as designed.
- A thicker-wall sanity check (10mm nominal instead of 3mm, same materials): `touch_temp_60s_C=22.2C` -- much cooler, correctly tracking wall thickness the same direction every prior version of this domain has shown, a real physical sanity check passed, not just "it ran."

**An honest, not-yet-resolved caveat, not glossed over**: the 3mm-wall case's `touch_temp_60s_C=80.7C` runs notably hotter than this domain's typical range for a similarly thin wall (§65-69's revolve oracle: ~57-72C for comparable thin shells). Suspected cause, not yet confirmed: `blockMesh`'s old revolve oracle could dedicate `N_RADIAL=12` cells specifically spanning JUST the wall thickness; this snappyHexMesh setup only specifies overall background-mesh + surface-refinement levels, and a 3mm gap can end up under-resolved by cells sized for the whole ~15cm domain unless a refinement region is explicitly added hugging the thin wall. The thicker (10mm) case's plausible result is consistent with this explanation (a bigger gap is easier for the same background resolution to resolve adequately). Flagged as a real open item, not silently accepted -- per the standing memory on not overindexing on any particular number, the fix here is about mesh-resolution correctness, not chasing a specific target temperature.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14).

**Open**: the thin-wall mesh-resolution concern above (likely fix: an explicit `refinementRegions` band hugging the wall surface, or a finer background mesh); Phase A2 (wiring into `generate_mug_dataset.py`, measuring a real accept/reject hit rate across a wide sampled parameter range); a real batch-throughput measurement (only single-candidate timing so far: 0.07s reject / 15-29s accept); Phase B (handle) and Phase C (joint) per the plan, untouched this section.

**§72 addendum -- the thin-wall concern confirmed directly, and a real second bug found along the way.** Direct user follow-up asked to actually verify (not assume) the through-wall resolution. Wrote `postProcess -func writeCellCentres` + parsed the solved `T` field's own internal values (not just boundary snapshots) at t=60s: a tight (theta,z) probe column at the §72 test case's "away from the bulge" location found only 4 cells, at essentially TWO distinct radial layers (r=30.38mm and r=31.28mm) -- a real, confirmed, coarse discretization, nowhere near `blockMesh`'s dedicated `N_RADIAL=12` in the old revolve oracle.

Investigating this surfaced a second real finding, not assumed: the "away from the bulge" location's ACTUAL local wall thickness was only 1.4mm, not the assumed 3mm nominal -- because the 2x2x3 FFD lattice used in §72's test (only 2 control points per cross-sectional axis) can only express a smooth GLOBAL linear ramp from a single corner's displacement, not a spatially localized bump (2 points per axis means every point IS a corner/extreme, so there's no "interior" neighbor to keep a bump local). Confirmed directly: thickness at that height ranged from 1.3mm to 9.3mm all the way around the ring, not a a localized 3mm-vs-bigger contrast as assumed when picking the probe location. A real, useful finding for calibrating expectations of the 2x2x3 lattice's actual expressive power (Phase A1's own choice, flagged there as "a tunable knob, not a fixed commitment") -- a genuinely LOCALIZED bump/feature needs more control points per axis (e.g. 4x4 or finer), which also directly bears on the thin-wall resolution question: some regions of an FFD-deformed shell may be much thinner than the base nominal thickness without that being visible from the input parameters alone, making a UNIFORM background mesh resolution risk under-resolving whichever region ends up thinnest, wherever that turns out to be.

Radiation was ALSO directly re-verified per the same user request (not just re-asserted): toggling `EMISSIVITY_SURFACE` 0.9->0.0 on the identical geometry moved `temp_at_2h_C` from 36.0C to 44.6C and `touch_temp_60s_C` from 80.7C to 82.4C, both in the physically correct direction -- confirms the radiative term is genuinely active in the v2 pipeline, not merely present in the written dictionary.

## 73. Food safety -- 9 of 20 structural materials removed, real reasons each

Direct user request: "let's make sure all the mug materials we're using/testing are food-safe." Went through all 20 `STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS` entries (the BODY material, the one in direct, sustained contact with ~90C liquid for up to 2 hours -- the actual constraint this domain's own physics already models) against what real products are actually made of BARE, not just what's thermally plausible:

**Removed (real reasons, not a rounding error)**: `polystyrene`/`abs_plastic` (not food-grade for hot contact, styrene/additive migration risk -- these are cold-drink-cup materials), `acrylic_pmma` (heat-deflection temp ~80-100C sits at or below this domain's own liquid temp held for hours -- a cold-beverage material, not hot), `melamine_resin` (explicit consumer-safety guidance against hot-liquid/microwave use, formaldehyde migration risk at temperature), `carbon_steel`/`cast_iron` (no real bare-metal drinkware use -- rust/leaching with sustained liquid contact), `brass`/`copper` (real drinkware in these metals -- Moscow mule mugs -- is always nickel/stainless-LINED, never bare; coffee's acidity accelerates leaching), `aluminum_6061` (real aluminum drinkware is anodized/lined; bare aluminum leaching is the standard reason it's avoided in practice).

**Kept, 11 materials, real spread k=0.22-17.0**: `polypropylene`, `nylon_pa6`, `soda_lime_glass`, `earthenware`, `borosilicate_glass`, `stoneware`, `fused_silica_glass`, `porcelain`, `bone_china`, `stainless_steel_304`, `titanium_grade2` -- still spans plastics/ceramics/glass/metal meaningfully, just without the specific materials real manufacturers don't sell bare for this exact use.

`HANDLE_MATERIALS` left untouched -- a handle doesn't contact the liquid, so food-contact-material standards don't apply the same way, and nothing there (woods, rubbers, common grip plastics, steel/aluminum) is a skin-contact hazard.

**Everything downstream picked this up automatically, confirmed not just assumed**: `mug_categorical.py`'s `CATEGORICAL_SPECS`/`MODEL_DIM` and `generate_mug_dataset.py`'s sampling bounds all derive `len(STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS)` dynamically rather than hardcoding 20 -- `MODEL_DIM` recalculated from 42 to 33 with zero code changes needed in either file. Only `viewer/src/pages/mug.astro`'s `STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS`/`STRUCTURAL_COLORS` JS arrays needed manual filtering (hardcoded, not derived from the Python table) to the same 11, same order. `mug_openfoam_oracle_v2.py` re-run with a material index now valid in the new 0-10 range (`bone_china`) to confirm it still resolves correctly post-filter: `valid=True`, plausible results.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14).

**Open**: this changes `struct_material_idx`'s valid range for BOTH the old v1 revolve pipeline and the new v2 FFD pipeline -- already-generated v1 datasets/trained models reference the old 20-material indices and are now stale (correctly premature to fix independently of the mug-v2 redesign already in progress, not a new problem this section created).

## 74. Coating options (glazes/enamel/liners) -- real physics, real reuse of a mechanism that was already there and already unused

Direct user request: "can we also add some kind of like... coating options? So we can handle things like glazes or whatever else is traditionally used?" Directly connects back to §73: a glaze is exactly WHY porous earthenware/stoneware are usable, food-safe, waterproof drinkware at all (raw fired clay is porous) -- this isn't an unrelated feature, it's the other half of the food-safety fix.

**Modeled as a thin EXTRA series thermal resistance, not a separately meshed region** -- real glazes/enamels are ~0.1-0.5mm, far too thin to mesh affordably at dataset-generation scale (would need an enormous local refinement given §72's own finding that even a 1.4-3mm wall is already under-resolved by the current background mesh). `externalWallHeatFluxTemperature` (the outer-surface BC, §67) already HAS a built-in mechanism for exactly this -- `thicknessLayers`/`kappaLayers`, present in every case since §65 but always written as empty `()`/`()`, unused until now. `liquidCoupling` (the custom inner-surface plugin, §66/§69) had no equivalent, so added one: a new `coatingResistance` dictionary entry (m^2 K/W, a precomputed thickness/k ratio -- only the ratio matters physically, no need for two separate entries the way the built-in mechanism has), folded into an `hEff = 1/(1/h + coatingResistance)` used everywhere `h_` previously was (both the liquid's own heat-loss integration and the Robin-BC valueFraction blend) -- same algebra `externalWallHeatFluxTemperature`'s own `totalSolidRes` uses, read directly from its source before writing this, not reinvented.

**`thermal_mug_spike.COATING_MATERIALS` (new, 3 entries)**: `ceramic_glaze` (k=1.1, covers majolica/tin-glaze/salt-glaze/most traditional pottery glazes -- thermally near-identical thin glass layers, so one representative entry rather than several cosmetically-different-but-physically-identical ones), `vitreous_enamel` (k=1.0, glass-based coating fused to METAL -- classic enamelware camping mugs), `epoxy_resin_liner` (k=0.2, a modern insulated-metal-mug liner, genuinely lower conductivity than the glass-based coatings, a real physical distinction not just a naming one).

**`mug_openfoam_oracle_v2.py`**: added `coating_material_idx`/`coating_thickness_mm` to `PARAM_NAMES` (now 78), applied identically to both `body_innerSurface` (via the new plugin field) and `body_outerSurface` (via the pre-existing built-in fields) -- matching how a real glaze covers a piece inside and out, not just one surface.

**Validated with two real comparisons, not just "it ran"**: a thin 0.3mm ceramic glaze on a 10mm earthenware wall showed a small, correctly-signed effect (`touch_temp_60s_C` 36.6C->36.1C, `temp_at_2h_C` 31.6C->31.8C -- both consistent with adding a small extra resistance, proportionally small since a thin glaze is a small fraction of a 10mm wall's own resistance). A deliberately exaggerated 2mm epoxy liner showed a MUCH larger, still correctly-signed effect (`touch_temp_60s_C` 36.6C->27.5C, `temp_at_2h_C` 31.6C->36.5C -- real insulation, real heat retention). Both directions and both magnitudes are physically sensible, not just nonzero.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14). Compiled `libliquidCoupling.so` rebuilt and re-baked into `Dockerfile.openfoam`'s image-build step (same mechanism as §66) -- no new per-candidate compilation cost.

**Open**: coating thickness/material currently applies uniformly to both surfaces with one shared parameter pair -- a real simplification (a genuine two-firing glaze process could in principle differ inside vs. out, but this matches how most real glazed pieces are actually made, a single dip/spray covering the whole piece); Phase B (handle) and Phase C (joint) from the mug-v2 plan are still untouched, as is Phase A2 (dataset-generation wiring) and the §72 thin-wall mesh-resolution fix.

## 75. Thin-wall mesh resolution -- a real fix, a real cost, and a real harness bug caught along the way

Direct continuation on §72's own open item. Bumped `refinementSurfaces` level on all four body patches from `(1 2)` to `(2 4)` as a first attempt -- too aggressive: `chtMultiRegionFoam` hit the 300s timeout entirely. Investigating that surfaced a real, separate bug in `openfoam_harness.run_tool()`: `subprocess.TimeoutExpired`'s own `.stdout`/`.stderr` can still be `bytes` even with `text=True` on the parent `subprocess.run()` call (a real, observed Python 3.8 quirk) -- the code unconditionally did `bytes + str`, raising a `TypeError` that MASKED the real `OpenFOAMError`/timeout message entirely. Fixed with a small `_as_str()` helper that decodes defensively regardless of which type shows up -- caught only because a genuinely slow solve actually exercised this path, not found by inspection.

Dialed back to level `(1 3)` and measured properly rather than guessing further: mesh grew from 29,087 to 138,090 cells (~4.7x), and the SAME tight-probe methodology from §72's addendum now finds 8 distinct resolved radial layers across the wall gap (a smooth 79.6C->78.0C gradient) instead of 2 -- a real, substantial resolution improvement. Cost: `chtMultiRegionFoam` alone took 207.6s for this candidate, up from the ~16-29s baseline -- a ~8-10x slowdown for ~4.7x more cells, i.e. worse than linear (consistent with PCG conditioning issues at the refined/unrefined cell-size transition, not just raw cell count).

**Tried and rejected a shortcut, not silently taken**: since implicit-Euler diffusion is unconditionally stable regardless of timestep size, tried `DT=10` (5x fewer steps) to recover some of the cost -- brought the solve down to 98.1s (a real ~2.1x speedup, not the full 5x, meaning per-step cost matters too) but degraded `touch_temp_60s_C` by 3.4C (76.3C vs 79.7C at `DT=2`) -- only 6 timesteps to resolve a thin wall's genuinely fast initial transient is a real accuracy loss, not a free one. `temp_at_2h_C` barely moved (31.51C vs 31.71C, the long-run value cares much less about early time resolution) -- confirming the degradation is specifically in the fast-transient target, not everywhere. Rejected: kept `DT=2.0`.

**Adopted**: `refinementSurfaces` level `(1 3)` (not `(2 4)`), `DT` unchanged at `2.0`. Real cost accepted for now (~207s/candidate for this test geometry, up from ~16-29s) since no dataset-generation throughput target has been set for the v2 pipeline yet (Phase A2, still open) -- correctness before scale, matching this whole redesign's own stated priorities. Not claimed as fully converged or as the final resolution choice -- a more surgical fix (a `refinementRegions` band hugging just the thin gap, rather than a blanket level bump across the whole surface) is a real, untried alternative that could recover some of this cost, flagged as open rather than assumed unnecessary.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14).

**Open**: the more surgical `refinementRegions`-based fix above, not attempted; real batch-throughput measurement at the new (slower) settings; Phase A2/B/C from the mug-v2 plan.

**Explicit decision, not a default**: user confirmed the 207.6s/candidate single-core cost is acceptable for now ("we can accept for now just to have something working, but we'll fix it later") after a direct check-in on whether that number was per-core or aggregate -- worth recording precisely, since none of our OpenFOAM invocations use `mpirun`/`decomposePar`/`-parallel` anywhere (confirmed by grep, not assumed): every `blockMesh`/`snappyHexMesh`/`chtMultiRegionFoam` call is a plain single-process run, on a host with 32 cores available. The per-candidate LATENCY (207.6s) is unaffected by that headroom, but dataset-generation THROUGHPUT is embarrassingly parallel across independent candidates -- up to 32x, i.e. ~6.5s/candidate-equivalent in aggregate, not 207.6s -- a materially different number for "can we afford to generate a dataset at this resolution" than for "is one candidate slow to iterate on." The more surgical `refinementRegions` fix remains a real, deliberately-deferred option, not abandoned.

## 76. Mug domain v2, Phase A2 -- real, non-trivial rejection confirmed at genuine batch scale, this whole redesign's own stated goal actually demonstrated

Direct continuation ("A2 next"). `scripts/generate_mug_dataset_v2.py` (new) -- the same accept/reject `run_batch_with_timeout` loop `generate_mug_dataset.py` already used for the old v1 revolve oracle (zero new harness code, exactly as the plan's own Context section predicted), pointed at `mug_openfoam_oracle_v2.py`. Deliberately sampled `r_inner_mm`/`r_outer_mm` from the SAME overlapping range (15-65mm), not `outer=inner+uniform(...)`, and all 72 lattice displacement components independently over +-5mm (no narrowing to protect the acceptance rate) -- the whole point of Phase A2 is confirming the sampler can and does produce genuinely infeasible designs, not tuning toward a comfortable hit rate.

**Result, a real batch, not a handful of hand-picked cases**: 192 candidates attempted, 52 accepted -- **27.1% hit rate**, real rejection at EVERY stage of the pipeline, not one gate doing all the work:
- 96 rejected by the cheap Python-side pre-check alone ("liquid cavity below minimum-viable-volume floor, or non-positive wall volume") -- no OpenFOAM invocation at all, confirming the fast gate is doing real work, not just theoretically present.
- 11 distinct self-intersection rejections spanning a genuinely wide severity range (23 to 242 locations) -- the surfaceCheck gate catching a real variety of different bad shapes, not one fixed failure mode.
- 33 rejected by `checkMesh` reporting quality failures -- the third, most expensive gate also doing real work, not redundant with the first two.

Total wall time 812.5s for 192 candidates at 32-way parallelism (n_workers=32, matching the confirmed 32-core host from §75's addendum) -- an aggregate throughput of ~4.2s/candidate-equivalent, even better than §75's back-of-envelope ~6.5s estimate, since the single most common rejection reason (non-positive wall volume) costs essentially nothing (no OpenFOAM invocation at all).

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14). Output written to `output/mug_v2_X.npy`/`_Y.npy`/`_feature_names.json`/`_target_names.json`/`_generation_stats.json` -- a real validation run's worth of accepted rows (52), NOT a production dataset (this script's own docstring says so explicitly -- Phase A2's goal was confirming the hit rate is real, not seeding training data).

**Open**: this was a validation-scale run (192 attempted), not a production dataset generation -- a real dataset would need many more accepted rows and a decision on whether to keep sampling `r_inner`/`r_outer` from the same wide overlapping range (high rejection, maximally honest about the feasible/infeasible boundary) or bias sampling toward a higher acceptance rate for actual training-data efficiency (a real, undecided tradeoff, not yet needed for Phase A2's own narrower goal); Phase B (handle) and Phase C (joint) from the mug-v2 plan, still untouched; the §75 mesh-resolution cost/surgical-fix tradeoff, deliberately deferred.

## 77. Mug domain v2, Phase B0 -- the handle as a real curved-arc rod, with a real attach-point-on-surface feasibility gate (the actual "floating donut" fix)

Direct continuation ("keep going"). This is the piece of the original user complaint §70 hadn't touched yet: *"I would much rather think like... we have a body and then we have ways we can attach... the representation now where the handle is a weird donut floating in space just like... doesn't help much."*

**Handle geometry (`mug_ffd_geometry.py` additions)**: a quadratic Bezier arc through 2 endpoints + 1 interior bulge control point (a real, standard curve, not novel), with a circular cross-section swept along it via a simple Gram-Schmidt frame (documented as a real simplification -- fine for a mostly-planar arc that never loops back on its own tangent, which is what every real mug handle is). `build_handle_stl()` writes a watertight `lateral`+`root`+`tip` STL, reusing the SAME `surface_triangles`/`write_stl` helpers the body already uses -- no duplicated tessellation logic. Spike-tested standalone before wiring in (same discipline as every prior phase): a realistic handle's own STL came back "Surface is closed... Number of unconnected parts: 1... Number of zones: 1" from `surfaceCheck` -- a valid, single-piece, consistently-wound tube.

**The real feasibility gate this phase exists for**: `nearest_surface_distance()`, a real point-to-tessellated-surface query (`scipy.spatial.cKDTree`, with a brute-force fallback) checking whether the handle's two actual attach points land within `ATTACH_TOLERANCE_M=10mm` of the body's own (possibly wildly asymmetric) outer surface. Calibrated directly before trusting it: a genuinely-touching attach point measured ~3.3mm (bounded by the body's own tessellation spacing, not exact contact), a deliberately floating one measured 100+mm -- an unambiguous, well-separated signal.

**Parameterization, a real design choice, not an oversight**: attach points are NOT free absolute (x,y,z) coordinates -- that would make a floating handle the overwhelming default outcome of random sampling (the body occupies a small region of a much larger free space) and demonstrate infeasibility while producing almost nothing else useful. Instead, a body-relative cylindrical frame (`theta`, `z_frac` of height, `radial_offset_mm` from the nominal `r_outer_mm`) keeps attach points GENERALLY near the body while `radial_offset_mm`'s own wide range (+-25mm) still leaves genuine floating/misses fully reachable -- the same overlapping-range philosophy §76 already used for `r_inner`/`r_outer`. `mass_kg` now includes the handle's own real mass (`pi*radius^2*arc_length*rho_handle`, arc length computed directly from the discretized centerline, not a formula assuming a straight rod).

**Validated with both a hand-picked case and a real batch, same discipline as every prior phase**:
- A realistic, correctly-attached handle: `valid=True`, `mass_kg` increased by a small, real amount over the body-only mass. A deliberately floating handle (offset +100mm): `valid=False`, `reason` reporting the exact measured distances (100.1mm, 100.1mm) -- both through the REAL `simulate()`, not just the standalone geometry spike.
- A real 960-candidate batch (`generate_mug_dataset_v2.py`, extended with the 9 new handle params): **30/960 accepted, a 3.1% hit rate** -- markedly lower than §76's body-only 27.1%, exactly as expected, since the handle-attachment gate compounds with the body's own constraints rather than replacing them. The rejection breakdown makes the new gate's own real contribution unambiguous: 436 rejected by the pre-existing wall-volume gate (unchanged by this phase), several dozen by self-intersection, and HUNDREDS of distinct `"handle attach point(s) not on body surface"` rejections, each carrying its own unique measured distance pair -- not one fixed failure mode repeating, genuine variety across the sampled range.

No test regressions (`pytest`: 14/14).

**Open**: the handle's own thermal physics (Phase B1 -- still no `handle_temp_60s_C`, and no back-effect of the handle on the body's own solve, matching the same staged sequencing §65->§68 used for the old revolve oracle); Phase C (joint) -- the handle currently has its own real material column but nothing thermal reads it yet, and there's no real "joint" distinct from "the two ends touch within tolerance"; the same production-vs-validation dataset-scale sampling-bias question from §76, now compounded by an even lower hit rate; the §75 mesh-resolution cost/surgical-fix tradeoff, still deliberately deferred.

## 78. Material library expansion -- structural, handle, and lining tables, direct user request ("expand the material library... coatings and materials and all that... linings as an option")

Pure `thermal_mug_spike.py` data-table work, deliberately not touching geometry/construction (§70-77's own active thread) -- confirmed first by reading `mug_openfoam_oracle_v2.py`'s existing `coating_material_idx` wiring and `mug_categorical.py`'s `MODEL_DIM` derivation so this addition wouldn't collide with either.

**STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS**: 11 -> 16. Same "real bare-food-safe product exists" bar §73 set, not a reconsideration of §73's own exclusions -- copper/brass/carbon-steel/cast-iron/bare-aluminum stay excluded for the same leaching/food-contact reasons (v6's FD oracle still has no coating layer to make a lined version of them physically honest). Added: `peek_polymer`/`high_density_polyethylene` (real hot-fill-rated food-grade plastics filling the gap between nylon and the glass cluster), `glass_ceramic`/`zirconia_ceramic` (real bare-ceramic bodies distinct from the existing clay-based earthenware/stoneware/porcelain/bone_china), `pewter_leadfree` (the real bare-metal drinkware category §73's exclusions correctly left out -- unlike copper/brass, solid pewter tankards are genuinely sold bare, not lined).

**HANDLE_MATERIALS**: 15 -> 22. No food-contact filter applies here (a handle never touches the liquid), so this is pure real-material coverage, not a standards question: `cork`/`leather_wrapped` (real traditional kettle/tankard grips), `phenolic_resin` (Bakelite-style, the actual classic heat-resistant handle material on real pots/kettles), `carbon_fiber_composite` (real modern premium handle material, isotropic-k approximated -- a real simplification, not assumed lossless, since no caller needs direction-resolved conduction through a handle this thin), `cast_iron`/`brass`/`copper` (real bare bail/handle metals -- legitimate here in a way they are NOT for `STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS`, since a handle carries none of the body's food-contact bar).

**COATING_MATERIALS**: 3 -> 7, and this table (not `STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS`) turns out to be the real answer to §73's own note that "real drinkware in these metals is always nickel/stainless-LINED, never bare" -- a copper-bodied mug is real specifically BECAUSE it's lined, so that's now expressible without reopening §73's bare-metal exclusion. Added `tin_lining`/`nickel_lining` (the historic and modern real linings for copper vessels), `silver_lining` (a real, if rare, heritage/luxury lining), `food_grade_lacquer` (a genuinely different real use case from the existing `epoxy_resin_liner` -- a thin cosmetic/protective spray coat, not a structural insulated-bottle liner). Indexed by `round()` in `mug_openfoam_oracle_v2.py`, not `material_props()`'s continuous interpolation, so unlike the other two tables these don't need to stay strictly k-ascending -- kept loosely ordered anyway for readability.

Every new row's (k, rho, cp) is a real published value for that specific named material (same Wikipedia-thermal-conductivity-list + Cengel/Incropera-appendix sourcing discipline as §62), nudged where needed to keep `STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS`/`HANDLE_MATERIALS` strictly k-ascending (the ordinal "higher index = more conductive" meaning `material_props()`'s interpolation and `mug.astro` both rely on) -- verified directly, not assumed: `ks == sorted(ks)` holds for both tables post-edit.

**Consequence flagged, not fixed here**: `mug_categorical.py`'s `MODEL_DIM` is derived from `len(STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS)`/`len(HANDLE_MATERIALS)` (35 -> 45, confirmed directly), so any existing `train_mug_cvae.py`/`train_mug_gan.py`/`train_mug_diffusion.py` checkpoint's one-hot material blocks are now the wrong width and need retraining against the expanded tables before they'll load -- the same natural consequence adding a real VMEC nfp value or a new domain has always had, not a bug. `COATING_MATERIALS`' growth has no such effect (never enters `mug_categorical.py` -- it's a v2-openfoam-only parameter, `len()`-bounds-checked dynamically wherever it's read).

Verified end-to-end on host (pure numpy/scipy, no Docker needed for this file): `material_props()` interpolates cleanly across both expanded tables at fractional/boundary indices, `gym_schema.mug_domain()` picks up the new `model_dim=45` automatically, and a full `simulate_v6()` call using the new last entries in both tables (`pewter_leadfree` body, `copper` handle) returns `valid=True` with physically sensible numbers -- `touch_temp_at_C=79.2` (correctly hot: pewter's real k=50 W/mK body barely resists conduction from a 90C fill, vs. earthenware's ~36C in earlier baselines) and `handle_temp_at_C=24.2` (correctly near-ambient: copper conducts well but the handle is thin/remote from the body's own heat). No existing test regression risk from this change beyond the flagged checkpoint-retraining need -- nothing in the (host-only) `pytest` suite covers the mug domain; the `openfoam`-service `pytest: 14/14` suite §65-77 reference covers `mug_openfoam_oracle*.py`/`mug_ffd_geometry.py`, neither of which this entry touched.

**Open**: retraining `train_mug_cvae.py`/`train_mug_gan.py`/`train_mug_diffusion.py` against the new `MODEL_DIM=45` (not done here -- a training run, not a materials-table edit, and this session's own scope was the material library specifically); whether `mug_openfoam_oracle_v2.py`'s coating BCs should eventually gate which `STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS` entries are legal without a nonzero `coating_thickness_mm` (e.g. requiring a lining for a hypothetical future copper/brass body) -- flagged, not decided, and deliberately left to whoever owns that oracle's construction thread; `INSULATION_MATERIALS` untouched (dead table -- v6 dropped the insulation-gap architecture entirely per this file's own v6 header, and no live oracle reads it), left that way rather than expanded for a design nothing simulates.

## 79. Material library, round 2 -- direct user request ("keep expanding it")

Same file, same discipline as §78: real, sourced, distinct materials only, `thermal_mug_spike.py` alone, nothing touching the active construction/geometry thread.

**STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS: 16 -> 19** -- `birch_wood`, `silicone_rigid`, `alumina_ceramic`. `birch_wood` is this table's first organic material -- a real bare-wood body (Nordic kuksa-style carved cups), which pairs with a new `beeswax_seal` COATING_MATERIALS entry (below) for the real traditional wax/oil food-safing treatment those cups actually use, rather than forcing a ceramic-glaze framing onto a wood body. `silicone_rigid` is the real, distinct rigid/collapsible food-grade-silicone cup category (rated near-boiling by real manufacturers) -- deliberately near-identical (k, rho, cp) to `HANDLE_MATERIALS`' `silicone_rubber`, since it is genuinely the same substance, same shared-triple convention as stoneware/stainless/titanium already used across tables. `alumina_ceramic` (96% Al2O3 technical ceramic) is a real bare ceramic distinctly more conductive (k=30) than every clay/glass-ceramic entry already here -- not thermally interchangeable with them.

**HANDLE_MATERIALS: 22 -> 25** -- `rattan_wrap` (real traditional woven-fiber grip wrap, distinct construction from cork despite a similar k), `glass_filled_nylon` (the real engineering-plastic kettle/appliance-handle material, distinctly more conductive than plain nylon_pa6 thanks to its glass-fiber loading), `titanium_grade2` (same substance/triple as the STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS entry -- real ultralight titanium bail handles on backpacking mugs).

**COATING_MATERIALS: 7 -> 9** -- `beeswax_seal` (this table's first non-mineral/non-metal entry, the real wood/earthenware waterproofing treatment `birch_wood` above needs), `gold_lining` (real, rarer heritage lining above `silver_lining`, completing a genuine luxury-lining tier).

Same verification discipline as §78, not skipped for round 2: `ks == sorted(ks)` reconfirmed on both ordered tables post-edit, all names still unique across all three tables, `mug_categorical.MODEL_DIM` now 51 (was 45, `9 + 19 + 25 = 53` was the naive expectation -- confirmed the actual dynamic derivation still just works, not hand-verified against a wrong arithmetic shortcut), `gym_schema.mug_domain()` picks up `model_dim=51` automatically, and a full `simulate_v6()` run on the new `birch_wood` body + `rattan_wrap` handle returns `valid=True` with `touch_temp_at_C=32.6` -- correctly cool to the touch, wood being a real insulator, a sharp contrast with §78's pewter-body check (`79.2`).

**Consequence, same as §78, now doubled**: `MODEL_DIM` moved a second time (45 -> 51) -- any checkpoint retrained against §78's 45-wide tables is ALSO now stale. Still not retrained here (same reasoning as §78: a training run, not a materials-table edit); whoever next retrains the mug generative models should do it once, against whatever `MODEL_DIM` this table settles at, not once per round.

**Open**: same three items as §78, all still genuinely open (the retraining itself; whether `mug_openfoam_oracle_v2.py`'s coating BCs should someday gate bare-metal `STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS` choices; `INSULATION_MATERIALS` staying a dead table on purpose). No new open items this round beyond the doubled retraining consequence above.

## 80. Material library, round 3 -- direct user request ("one more expansion"), and the last round for now

Same file, same discipline as §78/§79 -- real, sourced, distinct materials, `thermal_mug_spike.py` alone.

**STRUCTURAL_MATERIALS: 19 -> 21** -- `calabash_gourd`, `anodized_aluminum`. `calabash_gourd` is a second bare-organic vessel material distinct from §79's `birch_wood` -- specifically the real, still-widely-used dried gourd shell yerba mate is traditionally served in, and pairs with the new `sterling_silver` handle below (silver-rimmed mate gourds are a real, specific, well-known product). `anodized_aluminum` is the real, legitimate answer to §73's own bare-aluminum exclusion note: the anodized oxide layer is what makes aluminum drinkware/cookware food-safe in practice (hard-anodized camping mugs are a genuine common product), distinct from the untreated bare aluminum §73 correctly excluded -- shares `HANDLE_MATERIALS`' `aluminum_6061` triple exactly, since the anodize layer is microns thick and doesn't meaningfully change bulk conduction (same substance, same shared-triple convention as every other cross-table material here).

**Consequence, same shape as §78/§79, now three deep**: `MODEL_DIM` has moved a third time (45 -> 51 -> 55). Repeating the same flag as before, more insistently: any retraining of `train_mug_cvae.py`/`train_mug_gan.py`/`train_mug_diffusion.py` should happen ONCE, against the table sizes as they stand after this round, not once per expansion round -- three rounds of un-trained-against material growth is a real, accumulating gap between what the tables can express and what any generative model has ever seen, not a free accumulation.
